# Rulings — the decision-surface sitting (2026-08-31): how a decision surface must weigh its alternatives

> **Sitting record (Cowork writing side, 2026-08-31, the sixty-second session) — IN PROGRESS.**
> This record is the interim carrier (**D-230**); it lands in git at a later dispatch's Task 0.
> Nothing here is executed: no file of the record is edited, no code is touched, and **no register
> identity is allocated** — the register cannot accept one, and
> `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 0. The sitting's ground

The session delivered the first of four owed decision surfaces — the V4 divergence,
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_v4_divergence_surface_2026_08_31.md` — under the ruled form (D-249: the
whole surface as user-visible text first, the choice question in a separate later turn; one surface
per turn). **The user challenged it before the question was asked.**

## 1. The challenge, and it was upheld

**The user's words, verbatim: "It does not look as you weighed the alternatives against ultimate
objective(?)"**

**Upheld by this side, and two defects were found rather than one.**

1. **The stated axis was not used.** The surface's §6 labelled an axis *objective* and rated three of
   its four options on it as *"nothing improves now"* or *"the same as C in the short run"* — which is
   not a rating. **What it actually weighed was record correctness, and called that the objective.**
2. **★ The more serious one, found while repairing the first: the surface argued from an UNCHECKED
   CAUSAL CLAIM ABOUT OUR OWN SYSTEM.** Its §4 and §5 rested on the claim that an L1 detail
   specification would take the mis-stated figure *"as its ground for how strongly to weight
   tactus-level change points"*. **That is principle #18 (Class A)** — checkable, unchecked, and
   load-bearing — and `FRAMEWORK.md` §8.4 states the identical test for the identical shape of claim.
   **Checked at the record, it is false:** L1 publishes a *class* and does not judge (§5, §8.3), and
   the weight is fitted at L2 under DP-P and #20/**D-096**, with **#17f/D-431** forbidding
   hand-transcribed figures entering documents at all. **There is no path from the mis-stated figure
   to a fitted value.**

**The repair:** the surface gained a §6a carrying the weighing that was missing and naming the #18
defect; **the original ratings stand unedited (#12); the recommendation is unchanged and its reason is
replaced.**

## 2. THE RULING — what a decision surface must do with its alternatives

**The user's words, verbatim: "MAke sure decision alternatives are fact based and weighed against
ultimate objective and guiding principles. If they conflict, ultimate objective takes precedence."**

**The ruling, in three parts:**

- **(a) EVERY ALTERNATIVE IS FACT-BASED.** An option's pros and cons rest on established fact — read
  at a source, or derived at the record — and never on an asserted causal claim about our own system.
  **A claim about our own system that is checkable is checked before it carries load in a surface
  (#18), and one that cannot be checked is LABELLED as a reading rather than stated as a fact.**
- **(b) EVERY ALTERNATIVE IS WEIGHED AGAINST BOTH the ultimate objective — maximum-precision
  inference (#4) — AND the guiding principles.** Both, explicitly, per option. **A rating that says
  "nothing changes" on either axis is not a rating and does not discharge this.**
- **(c) ★ WHERE THE TWO CONFLICT, THE ULTIMATE OBJECTIVE TAKES PRECEDENCE.** This is the part the
  record did not previously carry: it fixes a precedence the existing rules leave open.

**What it sharpens rather than replaces.** **D-424** already required a surface to name the principle
behind every pro and con and to rate every option on two axes; **this names what the two axes are**,
and adds the fact-basis requirement and the precedence rule. **D-599** (apply the standing principles
first; what remains for the user is ratifying the derivation) is untouched and still runs first.
**D-658** (where the record does not settle the question, gather facts and make no recommendation) is
untouched.

**Why, in the ground the user has already put on the record.** Ruling 2 of 2026-08-30 widened this
pass's scope on exactly this argument, in his words: *"it is the entire architecture as a whole that
is evaluated against the ultimate objective, not separate pieces individually."* **A surface that
rates options on process hygiene rather than on the objective cannot serve that.**

## 3. ★ ONE QUESTION THE RULING RAISES, PUT BACK TO THE USER AND NOT ANSWERED HERE

Part (c) says the objective takes precedence over the guiding principles on conflict. **Read at its
widest, that could be taken as licence to override a SAFETY principle — #18 (no unverified causal
premises), #19 (no unestablished measurement tools, corpora, gates or figures), #20 (fit and
evaluation separated) — by asserting an objective benefit.**

**This side does not believe that is what was meant, and does not act on the wide reading**, for a
reason drawn from the principles themselves: **#18, #19 and #20 exist BECAUSE unestablished things do
not serve maximum-precision inference.** So a claimed conflict between the objective and one of them
is, in the ordinary case, **evidence that the objective claim is itself unestablished** — which is
precisely the defect §1 records this session committing.

**But the narrowing is not this side's to take** (D-643: a claim invoking a ruling quotes it in full,
never the branch that supports the claim). **The question is therefore recorded and put back:** does
part (c) reach the establishment principles, or does it govern only where the conflict is between the
objective and a principle of *method or form*? **Until it is answered, this side applies part (c) as
written and flags any case where the wide reading would change an outcome.** *No such case has arisen:
at the V4 surface the objective and the principles agree, so the precedence rule does not bite there
and was not exercised.*

## 3a. Ruling 2 — THE V4 DIVERGENCE IS CORRECTED MINIMALLY (Option A)

**The user's words, verbatim: "A — correct minimally (Recommended)"** — given upon
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_v4_divergence_surface_2026_08_31.md`, delivered across two turns and
amended under Ruling 1 before the question was asked.

**The ruling.** The clause at `FRAMEWORK.md` §5, L1, *"Why metric strength earns its place"* is
corrected to the primary's own three-level gradient — **71.5% of the strongest-level beats, 22.3% of
tactus beats, 2.4% of the level below (Temperley 2009, Table 1, Kostka–Payne corpus)** — with **the
former wording preserved in place (#12)**. **Option B is declined**: the primary's further finding —
that Temperley's model permits harmonic change only at tactus beats — **stays in the STOP memo and the
findings surface and does NOT enter the charter**, because a charter whose central structural
commitment is an exhaustive change-point set must not carry, in its own defense, the evidence for
another system pruning its candidates.

**The ground, as the surface stated it and the user ruled on it.** The objective and the principles
**agree**, so Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play. **#10's worth test catches the
issue on its (b) limb** — a specification that misdescribes is not *"correct and complete"* — and
**not** on the (a) limb the surface first claimed, which rested on the #18 defect §1 records.
**#1 and the theory-grounding corollary** require the `[FACT]` label to be earned at the paper.
**On the objective axis the value is to the framework's WARRANT** — the thing this pass was
commissioned to protect — and **no fitted value is at stake in either direction**, there being no path
from Temperley's table to a weight in our system.

**Execution, and it is not this side's act.** The correction is an edit to a tracked governing
document, so it goes in a **dispatch the user runs with CC** (the 2026-08-26 role ruling; **D-252**).
**No dispatch is written in this record.** Per **D-250** a dispatch is written when it is next, and
this correction's natural home is **the next batch that lands anything** — which is owed in any case,
three handoff entries being staged for prepend. **That batch's full scope depends on a ruling the user
still owes (the untracked population), so this side does not scope it here.**

**What it does NOT do.** It amends nothing by itself, moves no phase, authorizes no other edit to
`FRAMEWORK.md`, and touches no register, gate, corpus or measurement. **V4's STOP is discharged as a
decision and remains undischarged as an act until the dispatch runs.**

## 3b. Ruling 3 — DP-K's SECOND GROUND IS QUALIFIED IN PLACE AND THE ON-DOMAIN EVIDENCE IS ADDED (Option B)

**The user's words, verbatim: "B — fix wording, add the classical evidence (Recommended)"** — given
upon `ratification_surfaces/cowork_dpk_ground_surface_2026_08_31.md`, delivered in one turn and
restated in plain language in the next before the question was asked.

**The ruling.** At `FRAMEWORK.md` §9, DP-K's second ground is **narrowed to what its primary
supports** — one study, popular-music audio, systems scoring roughly ten percent above the annotator
agreement that study measured — with the 2015 result recorded beside it **and no contradiction
asserted between them**. **And the two on-domain findings are ADDED as further grounds**: that one
passage of classical music carries two defensible key readings at once, and that annotation
traditions differ enormously in which they record (one textbook marking a local key at 41.63% of
onsets against another at 15.97%). **Their read grades travel with them** — one RELAYED, one DECLARED
PARTIAL at chapter level — or the addition would import the very over-generalisation it removes.
Former wording preserved (#12).

**The ground.** **DP-K is CHOSEN and is not reopened**; its first ground — that the analyst writes the
alternative reading in the same stream, read at the object on this repertoire — is untouched. The
defect repaired is narrow and was stated precisely only after three of this side's earlier
characterisations were checked and withdrawn: **the two studies do NOT contradict** (different years,
corpora and system generations); **the framework does NOT conceal the domain bound** (R-1 declares it
globally); and **D-474's prohibition does NOT reach this use** (it bars off-domain agreement figures
as a *ceiling*, and DP-K uses this one as evidence about what a single-answer target measures).
**What remains, and what the ruling fixes, is a finding true of one study written as a settled fact
about the field** — which #1 and the theory-grounding corollary reach.

**On the two axes.** **They did not conflict, so Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play**
— recorded because Ruling 1 requires the question asked, not because it was close. **On the objective
axis the difference between all four options is small**, since nothing here changes what the analysis
computes; what differs is how well-defended a decision is that §7 calls *"the single most consequential
shape decision in this section"*. **The deciding reason was the pass's own purpose:** it was
commissioned to replace unread citations with read ones, and it found better evidence on this
project's own repertoire.

**Execution.** As with Ruling 2, this is an edit to a tracked governing document and therefore **a
dispatch the user runs with CC** (D-252). **No dispatch is written in this record.** Rulings 2 and 3
now both await the same batch.

**What it does NOT do.** It does not reopen DP-K, does not touch its first ground, authorizes no other
edit, and asserts no contradiction between the two studies. It touches no register, gate, corpus or
measurement.

## 3c. Ruling 4 — THE ROW-19 SECOND EXTRACTION IS PERFORMED (Option A). And a retraction preceded it.

**The surface:** `ratification_surfaces/cowork_row19_residual_surface_2026_08_31.md`. **The user's
words, verbatim: "ok, A. Do you need a new cowork session?"** — the ruling and a method question in
one line. **Answered: no** — the commission's own second route (*"a cleanly separated re-read that
does not consult the first extract"*) was available in-session and, on the reasoning at §5 of this
entry's row-19 block, **cleaner than a fresh session would have been.**

**★ THE RETRACTION THAT PRECEDED THE RULING, recorded here because it is the substance of the item.**
Three of the pass's files — the findings surface, the continuation file and the eighty-fourth handoff
entry — carried this side's sentence: *"the double pass exists to catch relay error, of which there is
none there."* **The commission gives the rule and no rationale at all.** That was an inference about a
rule's PURPOSE written into the record as the rule's CONTENT (**#18 Class A, #19**), and it is **the
second defect of that shape found this day** — the first being the one the user caught in the V4
surface at §1.2 above. **It was retracted in the surface, before the options were weighed**, and the
weighing was rebuilt on what the pass had actually measured: **three of the five error-classes its
cross-checks had caught are reader-class — omission, mis-attached table value, arithmetic — and an
at-the-object read has no immunity to any of them.**

**Executed the same evening.** Second extract and cross-check landed; **§8's double-extract condition
is now met literally for all eight central papers.**

**★ THE PREDICTION HELD.** Five defects found, **none a relay error**: the headline "configured"
column tuned per piece against its own ground truth with no held-out split (**#20**, and the first
pass had not raised it); Table 8.5's "Total" reported as a total when it cannot be one; precision and
recall never defined and no inter-annotator agreement figure (**#21, #24**); a verbatim quotation
silently improved (*"Tojo S"* for the page's *"Tojo T"*); and — caught by **neither** reader, only by
the cross-check at the page — **the chapter's own conclusion contradicting its body about this row's
most load-bearing fact**, which yields a standing routing bar on how row 19 may be cited.

**And the counterweight, which the ruling's record must carry too:** **no measured value moved**, the
row's load-bearing fact survived intact and was reached independently by both readers, and **the first
pass holds the row's single most useful synthesis, which the second did not reach.** **The doubling
improved the row's bounds and its citations. It did not change what the row says.**

**What it does NOT do.** It moves no design point, raises no STOP, and amends no document. **No
dispatch is owed by it** — the second extract and the cross-check are new untracked files, not edits
to tracked text — so Rulings 2 and 3 remain the only two awaiting a batch.

**★ AND IT CLOSES THE PASS'S USER-FACING WORK.** All four items are ruled. **The
first-deriving-subject decision now returns, deferred at Ruling 2 of 2026-08-30 — and choosing the
subject is the user's decision, not a session's.**

## 3d. Ruling 5 — THE ROW-2 / DP-D STOP IS HELD OPEN, AND TASK A CONTINUES (Option 3)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_five.md`, under D-249's form — the whole surface first, the choice
question in a separate later turn. **The user's words, verbatim: "Hold open, finish Task A"** — his
selection among the three options reproduced below.

**★ THE FORM DEFECT THAT PRECEDED IT, recorded because it is the same shape §1 of this record already
carries.** Asked *"What am I supposed to do?"*, this side answered with a compressed two-option
summary and, in the same message, the words to send to the executing session. **The user challenged
it: "THAT was a compressed answer, and not following rules for presenting decisions."** **Upheld.**
D-249 requires the whole surface as text the user has actually read BEFORE any choice question, and
for a consequential decision the question in a separate later turn; a decision answered blind is
voidable. **The surface was rewritten in full and the question asked in a later turn.** **This is the
second time in two days that a surface of this line failed the ruled form and the user caught it
before the question** — the first is §1 above, and both were caught by him rather than by this side.

**The item, stated so this record stands alone.** Task A's at-the-object re-read of population row 2
(McLeod & Rohrmeier 2024) **CORRECTED** a structural claim the findings surface used to reach a
rival-defused verdict under **DP-D** — which the remedial commission's §2 makes a falsifier candidate
and a STOP. The surface's claim was that the method *"does not move the input chord boundaries"*, and
the §3.1 coupling table placed the row at L3 on the ground that *"it consumes L2's output and revises
none of it."* **At the paper — read at its own pages by the executing session and independently
re-verified at those pages by this side:** §2.3, in the **method** section, *"Any remaining unmerged
vectors after this process are treated as separate chord labels, as they necessarily contain
incompatible PCs"*; §3.1.3, *"each merge only occurs in the model's input. The ground truth targets
remain unchanged: the goal is therefore that our method will leave such chords unmerged in its
output"*; and §3.1.4's measurement cutting windows wherever the output label changes. **Root, quality
and inversion are never revised — that half of the defusal holds. Boundaries are ADDED, by design.**
In this framework's own terms that is a revision of a decision L2 published: `FRAMEWORK.md` §5
publishes segmentation on **L2 → L3**, and **L3 → L2** is **"Nothing."**

**The three options as put.** **(1)** The finding corrects the pass's report and does not reach DP-D's
verdict; Task A resumes. **(2)** The finding reaches DP-D; the design point is reopened and Task A
stays stopped. **(3)** Hold the DP-D question open and let Task A finish, so the judgement is taken
against all seven re-reads rather than two of seven.

**THE RULING: option 3. DP-D is neither confirmed nor reopened.** Task A resumes on rows 3, 5, 17, 18
and 21. **The DP-D question is answered when all seven at-the-object re-reads are in.**

**★ THE STANDING OBLIGATION THIS CREATES, written down because the option's own recorded cost is that
it can be forgotten.** When Task A's seven rows are complete, **the DP-D question returns to the user
and is answered.** Until then **no session may read the held-open state as a defusal, as a
confirmation, or as licence to derive inside DP-D's frame.**

**The ground.** The record settles one half of the question and not the other, and the surface said so
rather than eliding it. **Settled at the file: DP-D's recorded ground — the analyst's own note at
§4.2, the C26 fact that duration weight cannot separate a long elaboration from a genuine added tone,
and the measured benefit running in both directions — does not rest on this paper**, whose only
appearance in DP-D's own text is none at all; its recorded rival is the first-running detector at
F .72. The 2024 paper enters as a fifth candidate shape **the pass itself added and defused**.
**Unsettled: whether that added analysis forms part of what the user took DP-D to stand on.** **On the
two axes of Ruling 1: they did not conflict, so the precedence clause did not come into play** —
recorded because Ruling 1 requires the question asked, not because it was close. On the **objective**
axis option 3 rates highest: #5 says gather facts where they may be scarce, and four of the five
untouched rows bear on neighbouring design points, one of them the pass's other rival-shaped row. On
the **principles** axis it rates mixed, because it reads the commission's own STOP clause — *"put it
to the user before continuing past it"* — more loosely than its words. **That cost is stated rather
than absorbed.**

**★ NO RECOMMENDATION WAS MADE, AND THE CONFLICT BETWEEN TWO RULES WAS NAMED RATHER THAN RESOLVED
SILENTLY.** D-249's form lists *"the recommendation with its reason"* as part of a surface; **D-658**
says that where the record does not settle the question, the surface returning it gathers facts and
makes **no** recommendation. This side applied D-658 to the unsettled half, **named both rules inside
the surface**, and offered to state a recommendation if the user read the precedence differently. **He
did not, and ruled without one.** **Which of the two governs a surface of this kind is not settled by
this ruling and is not settled here.**

**What it does NOT do.** It takes **no verdict on DP-D**. It amends no document: the findings
surface's DP-D bullet and its §3.1 coupling row stay exactly as delivered, and their correction
remains the user's act on a surface put to him. It does **not** annotate the row-2 cross-check, whose
settlement against the second pass this re-read found to be the weaker reading — **the second such
case in this pass**, row 1's ±14 line-of-fifths bound being the first, and a finding about the
doubling exercise rather than about either paper. It moves no phase, touches no register, gate,
corpus or measurement, and authorizes no derivation.

**Execution.** **No dispatch is owed by it.** The executing session records the ruling in its own STOP
file and continues; that is a new untracked file, not an edit to tracked text. **Rulings 2 and 3 no
longer await a batch** — both were executed this same day by
`cc_instruction_reading_pass_landing_second_2026_08_31.md`, and this side verified both corrections at
`FRAMEWORK.md` §5 and §9 after that batch reported.

## 3e. Ruling 6 — DP-D's DEFUSAL IS REPAIRED **AND** CARRIES ITS ESTABLISHMENT BOUND (Option C)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_five.md`, in the ruled form, the choice question in a later turn. **The
user's words, verbatim: "Agree with C"**.

**Ruling 5's condition is met.** Task A is complete — seven rows, every one read at the object as page
images, **61 load-bearing structural claims confirmed, 8 corrected, 3 not establishable, and no
falsifier against any chosen design point in any row**
(`reading_pass/object_reads/task_a_consolidated_2026_08_31.md`). The DP-D question therefore returned
to the user, as §3d required.

**What was NOT decided, stated first because it is the larger fact. DP-D's chosen position —
chord-tone assignment inside L2's one decision — does not move, and no option put to the user moved
it.** No row of the seven supplies a measurement against it.

**The facts the ruling was taken on, each established at the object.** DP-D's ratified ground is the
analyst's own note at §4.2, the C26 fact that duration weight cannot separate a long elaboration from
a genuine added tone, and the benefit measured running in both directions; its recorded rival is **the
first-running detector at F .72**, and the 2024 paper appears in DP-D's own text **nowhere**. The pass
added a fifth candidate shape — an L3 read-off augmenting L2's published output — and defused it on
three legs. **Leg one is false at the paper:** the method subdivides the span it is handed and doing
so is its stated goal (§2.3 **in the method section**, §3.1.3, §3.1.4, and two boundary errors at
p. 10); root, quality and inversion are indeed never revised. **Leg three is false:** the paper argues
for its shape twice and measures a benefit of splitting the task. **Leg two survives and is the one
that carries: no one measures post-hoc assignment against joint assignment.** The rest of the
evidence base is one fenced representation (row 3 — a paper with no inference procedure and not one
measured number), one absence (row 1 publishes no chord-tone assignment and names it future work),
and four rows with nothing.

**THE RULING: Option C, both halves.** The **factual corrections** land — §2's DP-D bullet, and the
§3.1 coupling table's placement of row 2 at *"L3 — it consumes L2's output and revises none of it"*,
which is wrong at the paper on every reading. The **exclusion ground is restated**: the fifth shape is
not one anything read exhibits, because the only published instance re-cuts the spans it was handed,
which §5's **L3 → L2: Nothing** contract forbids and DP-M narrows. **And the restated ground carries a
DECLARED BOUND: it rests on a reading of our own contract and not on evidence — no measurement
anywhere compares post-hoc assignment against joint.**

**The ground, and a recommendation was given rather than withheld.** Unlike §3d, **the record settles
this question, so D-658 does not apply**; this side recommended Option C with its reason and the user
agreed. **On the two axes of Ruling 1 the options differed on both, so neither rating was vacuous.**
On the **principles** axis, Option A is exposed on **#19** — a conclusion reached through two false
premises is not established because a third survives — Option B is what #19 says, and C carries both
with a small **#6** duplication risk. On the **objective** axis the difference is the **warrant a
deriving session inherits**: under A the post-hoc shape reads as closed when what closes it is a
contract reading; under C the next reader meets the corrected fact and the limit on what it
establishes in the same place. **They did not conflict, so Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come
into play.**

**The deciding reason, measured in this pass rather than argued.** Row 5's cross-check wrote a caveat
explicitly *"so the findings surface meets the caveat at the same moment it meets the claim, rather
than inheriting the stronger reading alone."* **Neither DP-B's bullet nor DP-E's carries it, and DP-E
strengthened the claim further by adding "was measured."** The claim arrived; the bound did not. A and
B each carry half of what is true here, and **C is the only option that puts both halves where the
next reader meets them** — **#24**'s principle, that a result travels with its bound, applied to a
verdict rather than to a figure.

**★ A CORRECTION THIS SIDE MADE TO THE CONSOLIDATED FILE'S OWN REASONING, recorded because the order
of business rested on it.** That file states the DP-D choice *"falls to the establishment principles
(#18, #19), which is the open question that ruling itself left with the user"* — meaning §3 above.
**The clause does not follow.** §3's open question is whether the objective's precedence may
**override** the establishment principles; the same file establishes that the objective is served
**identically** by both readings, so there is no conflict, the precedence clause never bites, and §3
need not be answered to decide DP-D. **What DP-D needed was #18 and #19 APPLIED, which is ordinary.
§3 remains open and untouched by this ruling.**

**What it does NOT do.** It does not reopen DP-D and does not move its chosen position. It authorizes
no derivation, no design and no inference change. **It does not decide the rest of what Task A leaves
owed** — five further corrections to the findings surface, one to §5's routed extracts, four to
cross-check files, and the disposition of the finding that **five of the seven rows produced an item
where a doubled reading settled a question the wrong way or cited a source that is not at the
citation**. It touches no register, gate, corpus or measurement, and it does not answer §3.

**Execution.** The findings surface was tracked in git by
`cc_instruction_reading_pass_landing_second_2026_08_31.md` earlier this day, so its correction is an
edit to a tracked document and therefore **a dispatch the user runs with CC** (**D-252**). **No
dispatch is written in this record** (**D-250**: a dispatch is written when it is next). Its natural
home is the next batch that lands anything, together with the other corrections Task A leaves owed.
**`FRAMEWORK.md` is not edited by this ruling** — DP-D's own text never carried the corrected claim.

## 3f. Ruling 7 — PART (c) DOES NOT REACH THE ESTABLISHMENT PRINCIPLES, AND THE ESCALATION ROUTE IS NAMED (Option C)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_six.md`, under D-249's form — the whole surface as user-visible text
first, with no choice question in the same turn. **The user's words, verbatim: "Agree on C"**, given
upon the four alternatives reproduced below. **§3's open question is CLOSED by this ruling.**

**THE RULING, in two halves.** **(i)** Part (c) of Ruling 1 governs a conflict between the ultimate
objective and a principle of **method or form**. **It does not reach #18, #19 or #20: no decision
surface may set an establishment principle aside by asserting an objective benefit.** **(ii) The
escalation route is NAMED:** where a session believes an establishment principle is genuinely costing
the objective, it **surfaces that to the user as its own decision, with its evidence**, and never
resolves it by applying precedence itself. **The ruling binds what a SURFACE may do in its own
weighing. It does not bind the user**, whose ruling on a named case remains available — which is the
half Option A lacked and the reason C was recommended over it.

**The interim rule recorded at §3 is SPENT.** That rule — *"this side applies part (c) as written and
flags any case where the wide reading would change an outcome"* — was written to hold only until this
question was answered. It is answered.

**The four alternatives as put.** **(A)** Narrow: part (c) reaches method and form only. **(B)** Wide:
part (c) reaches every principle, establishment principles included. **(C)** Narrow, with the
escalation route named. **(D)** Leave open and keep §3's interim rule.

**The ground, on the two axes Ruling 1 requires.** **The options differed on both axes and neither
rating was vacuous.** On the **objective** axis, A and C rate highest on the user's own recorded
ground at **D-675** — an establishment obligation gates whatever its subject *"because trust in a
measurement is trust in the analysis"* — so a claimed conflict between the objective and #18/#19/#20
is ordinarily evidence that the objective claim is itself unestablished, which is the defect §1 of
this record catches. **B rates lowest, and its cost is measured rather than argued:** row 7's Option B
was declined on **#19** over a resemblance two records had named the likely answer, and the user then
supplied the document proving the resemblance wrong — a real and distinct primary would have been
dropped from the population with no mechanism to find out (§4 of this record); and row 19's retraction
on #18/#19 preceded an exercise that found five defects the retracted premise would have kept (§3c).
**D rates neutral today and latent tomorrow:** the clause has never bitten — §3a, §3b, §3d and §3e each
record that the two axes did not conflict, and §3c states the question neither way — so leaving it open
costs nothing until the first case that does bite, which would then be decided by whichever session met
it, under a rule this record says in the same paragraph it does not believe. On the **principles**
axis, **B is exposed on #19 directly**, whose own words are that a thing is trusted *"never because it
is merely unfalsified"*; A and C are consistent with two of the user's own rulings that had the
opportunity to weaken #19 and explicitly preserved it — the 2026-08-11 apparatus lapse rule, which
accepted a residual #19 exposure while stating that an establishment obligation never lapses, and
D-675's finish-line cut, which encodes the carve-out rather than leaving it to be remembered. **C
carries a small #6 duplication risk**, its escalation sentence arguably already borne by #13 and by the
standing rule that a session takes no decisions; that cost was stated in the surface and the user ruled
with it in view. **The two axes did not conflict, so part (c) did not come into play in deciding its
own scope** — recorded because Ruling 1 requires the question asked, not because it was close.

**A recommendation was given rather than withheld**, on the test §3e applied: **D-658** governs only
where the record does not settle the question, and here it largely does, so **D-599** runs — the
standing principles decide, and what remained for the user was ratifying the derivation and judging
whether C's escalation sentence earns its place against #6.

**What it does NOT do.** It changes nothing in Ruling 1's parts (a) and (b), which were not in
question, and does not re-open Ruling 1. **It re-opens no surface already ruled**, and it does not
revisit Rulings 2 through 6 — none of them exercised the precedence clause, so none turns on this
answer. It authorizes no fix, no design, no derivation and no inference change; it moves no phase and
no gate; it touches no register row, no register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool and no measured
value. **It does not answer the separate question §3d left open** — whether D-249's recommendation
clause or D-658's no-recommendation rule governs a surface of that kind.

**Execution. No dispatch is owed by it.** Ruling 1 lives in this record and nowhere in a tracked
governing document, so nothing tracked misdescribes anything until this record itself lands, which it
does at a later dispatch's Task 0 (**D-230**). **No register identity is allocated** — the register
cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route, exactly as this
record's own header states.

## 3g. Ruling 8 — THE GRAIN OF A UNIT IS THE DECISION (Option A). `FRAMEWORK.md` §9.0 IS SETTLED.

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_six.md`, under D-249's form — the whole surface as user-visible text
first, the choice question in a later turn. **The user's words, verbatim: "Agree on A"**. The surface
**re-derived** the parked draft at
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_first_deriving_subject_surface_2026_08_31.md` §3 in this session's own
name rather than putting a question on that draft, which is what the eighty-fourth entry required of
the phase's next session.

**THE RULING. `FRAMEWORK.md` §9.0 is settled at its second candidate reading: a UNIT of this
architecture is a DECISION the analysis makes about the music** — §0's own definition, *"One question
about the music that the analysis settles, and could settle differently."* **A factor is not a unit**;
§0 already states it is *"a means of computing a decision, never a decision."* **The factor roster and
the terms that rate candidate readings stay where §5's L2 charter and DP-P already place them —
inside L2's detail specification, derived within that charter.** **Options B and C are declined**, and
**Option D — the decision reading plus a framework-level obligation that L2's detail specification
carry its independence premises labelled under #17(a) — was named, rated and declined on #6**, that
obligation already binding through #17(a) and R-5.

**What it fixes, operationally.** The detail-specification phase writes at the grain of the layers and
components as the charters already stand. **It does not re-cut the layers, re-open any charter, or
change any boundary contract**, and in particular the boundary contracts remain published-fact
contracts rather than independence claims — which is precisely what the factor reading would have
turned them into, by §9.0's own account of itself.

**The ground, on the two axes Ruling 1 requires. The options differed on both and neither rating was
vacuous.** On the **objective** axis, A rates highest because the decisions are what the ground truth
records, what two independent analysts disagree about, and what the analysis must publish — so
specifying at that grain is specifying the thing that is measured, while nothing is given up, the
rating terms being specified one level down where the charter already assigns them. **B rates lowest,
and its cost is concrete:** it pulls back up content §5 pushed down deliberately, and it makes the
framework's own load-bearing claims a set of roughly ten conditional-independence premises **that
nothing read measures** — the pass having established that nothing read models an analysis as a factor
roster at all. **C rates middling on a thinner support than it appears to have:** the one work that
exhibits the reconciliation shape was re-read at the object in Task A and is *"a paper that contains no
inference procedure and not a single measured number"*, with three of its items found not establishable
because supplementary material had been cited as the paper — so the shape is exhibited as a
representation and carries **no evidence about inference** (**#19**), while its doubled unit count and
two-tiered charters are paid immediately and certainly. On the **principles** axis, A is consistent with
§0's glossary, §5's placement, DP-P, §8.3's rule that style enters through priors and weights and
**never through the structure**, and #7's reading of a layer as a responsibility; **B is exposed on #18**
— roughly ten checkable causal claims about our own system, unchecked at the moment of writing, which
**Ruling 7 of this record forbids trading against an asserted objective benefit** — and would require
amending the ratified glossary; **C carries B's exposure nested inside A's structure and adds a #6 cost**,
two homes per concern. **The two axes point the same way on all four options, so they did not conflict
and Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play** — recorded because Ruling 1 requires the
question asked, not because it was close.

**A recommendation was given rather than withheld**, on §3e's test: **D-658** governs only where the
record does not settle the question, and here the standing material largely does, so **D-599** runs —
what remained for the user was ratifying the derivation and judging whether Option D's sentence earned
its place.

**★ A WORDING DEFECT IN THE SURFACE, CAUGHT BY THE USER BEFORE THE QUESTION AND RECORDED RATHER THAN
QUIETLY EDITED.** The surface headed its two weighing sections *"Against the ultimate objective"* and
*"Against the guiding principles"*. **The user challenged the wording: *""Against the ultimate
objective" in a negative sense…?"*** **Upheld.** *Against* is a two-place word carrying both *measured
against* and *in opposition to*, and the headings had dropped the verb that fixes which — the writing
standard's own case, the multiple-meaning rule of `cowork_design_doc_template.md`. Read as opposition,
the headings announce that every option is being argued down, which is the reverse of what those
sections do. **The headings were corrected to *"Each option on the objective axis"* and *"Each option
on the principles axis"*, the phrasing this record already uses, and NO rating, ground or
recommendation moved** — stated at the time, because a correction arriving mid-surface is the moment a
reader cannot otherwise tell a repair from a revision. **It was caught by the user's reading and not by
this side's self-check**, which is the third consecutive sitting at which a surface of this line was
corrected by him before its question, after §1 and §3d.

**What it does NOT do.** It authorizes **no derivation, no design and no inference change** — the first
deriving subject is a separate decision and is still the user's. **It moves no design point:** DP-N,
DP-O and DP-Q stay underived, and no chosen design point is reopened. It does not decide how L2's terms
are formed, combined or fitted. It touches no register row, no register entry, no corpus, no
measurement tool and no measured value. **It settles §9.0 and nothing else**; R-6's other three items
stand exactly as they are.

**★ WHAT IT UNBLOCKS.** §9.0 stood in front of the first-deriving-subject decision by the framework's
own word — *prior* — and that obstacle is now removed. **The ordering decision is next and it is the
user's.**

**Execution. A DISPATCH IS OWED AND IS NOT WRITTEN HERE** (**D-250**: a dispatch is written when it is
next; **D-252**: the user runs it with CC). Two statements of a tracked governing document are false at
HEAD once this ruling stands, and both are the filing convention's **branch two** — a live governing
surface, whose body is corrected with the former wording preserved in place (**#12**): **§9.0's heading
and closing sentence**, which say the question is put to the user and not settled here, and **§11's
R-6**, which lists §9.0 among the items awaiting a ruling. **No other edit to `FRAMEWORK.md` is
authorized by this ruling.** Its natural home is the batch already carrying Ruling 6's corrections and
the rest of what Task A leaves owed, whose scope is the user's. **No register identity is allocated** —
the register cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3h. NOT A RULING — the compaction question is CLOSED BY DECLARATION, and the bound is carried

**The item.** The eighty-fifth handoff entry raised, and left unanswered, whether the compaction of the
reading pass's session fell before or after Task 4 was written — that is, whether
`cowork_reading_pass_findings_2026_08_31.md` was written by an author who could still see its own
boot. It recorded the position correctly: **unestablished, and under #18 not to be assumed either
way.** **The user was asked in this sitting and his words are: "cannot remember unfortunately".**

**What the record was found to hold, checked before the question was put.** No mention of a compaction
exists anywhere in `reading_pass/` — not in `population.md`, not in `continuation.md`, not in the
findings surface itself; the only two records of it are the parked first-deriving-subject draft's
banner and the eighty-fourth handoff entry. **But the eighty-fourth entry carries a bearing statement
the eighty-fifth's question did not take into account.** Describing the moment the user asked whether
to continue in the same session — by that entry's own sequence after the seven cross-checks and the
row-19 read, and immediately before Task 4 — it records the reason given for continuing: *"the cadence
rule's own mechanism — the context ceiling that compacted the fifty-ninth session — was not in play at
roughly 99% of context remaining"*, followed by *"Task 4 was then written and landed."*

**Why that narrows the question without settling it, stated at both limbs.** If the claim is true the
compaction fell after Task 4. It is nevertheless **a session's statement about its own conditions,
which #18's own clause makes DECLARED and not establishable** — *"not checkable from outside that
session"*. And whether a reading of 99% remaining excludes an EARLIER compaction depends on how the
tooling reports remaining context after one, **which was not checked and is not assumed in either
direction**, that being the Class-A shape this very sitting has already caught twice.

**THE CONSEQUENCE, which follows from the principles and is not a choice.** The condition cannot be
established at an inspectable object, so under **#24**'s second half it is **DECLARED as a bound and
the result stands with the bound attached**. The bound, stated once so it can be quoted: *whether the
findings surface's derivation had its own session-start boot in view is unestablished; the user was
asked and does not recall; the eighty-fourth entry's contemporaneous context claim points to "after
Task 4" and is itself declared rather than established.*

**What rides on it, and it has shrunk since the question was raised.** Task A re-read seven of that
surface's rows **at the object**, confirming 61 load-bearing structural claims, correcting 8 and
finding 3 not establishable
(`reading_pass/object_reads/task_a_consolidated_2026_08_31.md`). The surface's central readings have
since been checked by a different session, so the bound bears on progressively less of it. **That is a
reduction in exposure, not a discharge:** the bound stands.

**What is owed and NOT scoped here.** Under #24 the bound belongs **attached to the result**, which
means on the findings surface — a tracked document, so a dispatch the user runs with CC (**D-252**),
and **D-250** puts its writing at the point it is next. Its natural home is the batch already carrying
Ruling 6's corrections and the rest of what Task A leaves owed. **That batch's scope is the user's and
is not set here.** Nothing else is owed by this item, and **no register identity is allocated**.

## 3i. Ruling 9 — THE ROW-5 DEPARTURE IS RATIFIED, AND THE COMMISSION'S TWO STOP WIDTHS ARE RECONCILED (Option B)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_six.md`, under D-249's form, the choice question in a later turn. **The
user's words, verbatim: "Agree on B"**.

**★ THE FINDING THAT RESHAPED THE QUESTION, established at the commission's own text before the
options were framed. `cowork_reading_pass_remedial_commission_2026_08_31.md` states its STOP at TWO
DIFFERENT WIDTHS.** §2, the task instruction: *"a CORRECTED verdict on a claim the findings surface
used to reach a **SUPPORTS** or **rival-defused** verdict. **That is a falsifier candidate and it is a
STOP** — write it up on its own and put it to the user before continuing past it."* §5, the section
headed *STOPs — surface, never absorb*: *"A CORRECTED structural claim **that moves a design-point
verdict**…"* **Row 5's correction fires the first and not the second** — it sits under a SUPPORTS
verdict, and it moves no verdict, DP-B and DP-E resting primarily on V2 and V7, both verified at the
object from another source. **So whether a STOP fired at all was unsettled at the governing text, not
merely unruled**, and the departure existed only under one of the two readings. Both clauses were
quoted in full to the user rather than the one supporting a verdict (**D-643**).

**THE RULING, in three parts.** **(i)** The **broad trigger governed and a STOP DID fire**: the
continuation past it was a real departure. **(ii) The departure is RATIFIED after the fact**, on the
ground the executing session gave — the user's row-2 ruling ordered five further rows run and the
DP-D question answered over all seven, and halting on each corrected claim would have defeated the run
he ordered. **(iii) The two clauses are RECONCILED going forward rather than one being retired:
writing the finding up on its own and surfacing it to the user is UNCONDITIONAL and IMMEDIATE (§2);
the HALT is owed only where the correction could move a design-point verdict (§5).** The surfacing
obligation is the half that keeps nothing absorbable, and it is deliberately not made to depend on a
reading session's own judgement of whether a verdict has moved.

**AND THE SUBSTANTIVE CORRECTIONS LAND.** At the object, over all seventeen printed pages, the claim
the findings surface carries is exact in its quoted clause and wrong in its status: p. 45 reads
*"Although adding a modulation specification to the model is straightforward, this **would** quickly
lead to an avalanche of ambiguous solutions … the total number of ambiguous analyses quickly explodes,
**given the rules of the previous section**"* — conditional verbs, the authors' stated reason for
**not** building modulation, and the paper's only experiment measuring the shipped modulation-free
grammar. **So *"attempting … made"* (§2, DP-B) and *"was measured"* (§2, DP-E) each attribute an
experiment the paper does not report.** Three edits are owed to
`cowork_reading_pass_findings_2026_08_31.md`: **both §2 bullets restated to an implementer's stated
expectation bounded to his own rule set**; **§5's routing kept but carrying that bound**; and **the
cross-check's caveat carried beside the claim** — that the explosion is in the number of ambiguous
analyses a grammar admits, *"not in the cost of a probabilistic decode over a bounded state space, so
whether the lesson carries to a decode is not settled by this paper"*. **That caveat was written down
by the second reader and did not survive the step into the surface** — the same transfer failure that
decided Ruling 6, and #24's principle that a result travels with its bound, applied for the second
time. *Recorded because it cuts the other way and the record should not carry only the flattering
half:* the word *"unconstrained"* **understates** the paper rather than overstating it, the claim
being made *"even with a constrained modulation specification"*.

**The ground, on the two axes Ruling 1 requires. The options differed on both and neither rating was
vacuous**, the factual corrections being common to all four and the difference lying in what evidence
base a deriving session inherits and at what cost in the user's turns. On the **objective** axis, B
rates highest **on this pass's own measured numbers**: Task A's consolidated table marks **three** of
its nine items as meeting §2's trigger — two on row 2, one on row 5 — and **none of the three moved a
design point**, so a halt on each would have spent three of the user's turns and delayed the remaining
rows for nothing, while the seven-row run is precisely what produced Ruling 6's DP-D judgement. Option
C, the strict reading, therefore rates lowest on this axis and runs against **#5**. Option A rates
well on outcome and weakly on mechanism: declaring the broader clause loose would leave the next
commission with only the narrow trigger, which is judged by the reading session itself — the party
least able to see that a verdict has moved. Option D rates poorest, an unresolved trigger being
decided ad hoc by whichever session meets it next. On the **principles** axis, B takes **both** clauses
at their words and gives each a distinct job, which is what **#12** asks of two texts that say
different things, and it is **#13** applied at its own stated purpose — *surface a surprise as a STOP
before building around it* — nothing having been built around this one. **Its cost is declared rather
than absorbed: the reconciliation is a narrowing this side DERIVED and the commission's text does not
state it**, so what the user ratified is a derivation and not a reading off the page. A rates cleanly
on **#6** but resolves a conflict by declaring one of two ratified clauses loose. C is strongest on
§2's letter and weakest on #5. D leaves a checkable ambiguity in a governing instruction unchecked,
which is #18's shape applied to a procedure. **The two axes point the same way on all four options, so
they did not conflict and Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play** — recorded because
Ruling 1 requires the question asked.

**A recommendation was given rather than withheld:** the record settles the substantive half outright
and supplies both clauses and the count of what each would have cost on the procedural half, so
**D-658** does not apply and **D-599** runs.

**What it does NOT do.** **It moves no design point.** DP-B and DP-E keep their footing, which rests on
V2 and V7 verified at the object from another source, and no chosen design point is reopened.
**`FRAMEWORK.md` is not touched, and this was established at the file rather than assumed:** the
parse-space claim appears nowhere in it, so the corrections are confined to the findings surface. It
authorizes no derivation, no design and no inference change. **It does not reopen the reading pass**,
whose commission is spent, and it does not revisit any other row. It touches no register row, no
register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool and no measured value.

**Execution.** The three corrections are edits to a tracked document and therefore **a dispatch the
user runs with CC** (**D-252**); **no dispatch is written here** (**D-250**). Their natural home is the
batch already carrying Ruling 6's corrections and the rest of what Task A leaves owed, **whose scope is
the user's and is not set here.** **No register identity is allocated** — the register cannot accept
one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3j. Ruling 10 — THE FIRST DERIVING SUBJECT IS L0+L1, THEN L2 (Option B)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, under **D-249**'s form — the whole surface as user-visible text
in one turn, the choice question withheld to a later one. **The user's words, verbatim: "L0+L1 first,
then L2"**. This is the decision Ruling 2 of `cowork_rulings_2026_08_30_detail_phase_opening_sitting.md`
deferred in its own words — *"the first-deriving-subject decision of the earlier surface is DEFERRED,
not decided — it returns after the findings surface is in."*

**★ WHAT MADE IT ASKABLE, AND IT IS RULING 8'S DOING.** The parked draft at
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_first_deriving_subject_surface_2026_08_31.md` carried **three** options,
and its recommendation was **A then B** — rule §9.0 first, then derive L0+L1. **Ruling 8 settled §9.0**,
and with it Option A is **SPENT**: the whole case for A was that an unsettled §9.0 could reshape every
charter by that section's own words. **§8 of that draft also left open whether the objective's
precedence reaches #18, #19 and #20; Ruling 7 answered it**, and the draft had already recorded that
its outcome did not turn on that answer. So the live choice was **two**, and it was put as two.

**★ THE DRAFT WAS RE-DERIVED IN THIS SESSION'S OWN NAME, WHICH ITS OWN BANNER REQUIRED.** That banner
states the draft rests on a boot its author was compacted out of and *"must either re-derive it or read
it as a witness and endorse it in its own name before any question is put."* This session booted in
full and uncompacted, and re-read at the files: `FRAMEWORK.md` §5 whole (the L0 contract, the L1, L2 and
L3 charters, the second axis, the boundary contracts) and §9 whole (§9.0 and DP-A…DP-Q), and
`cowork_reading_pass_findings_2026_08_31.md` §1, §2 at §9.0 and DP-N, DP-O, DP-Q, §3.2 and §3.3.

**★ ONE CORRECTION TO THE DRAFT, CARRIED RATHER THAN REPEATED (#12).** The draft states that the
framework has **four** design points marked *NONE CHOSEN — underived*, of which three sit in or against
L2's path. **At the file there are three so marked — DP-N, DP-O and DP-Q — and all three are L2's.**
DP-P carries a different marking, *NOT DECIDED HERE — detail specification and measurement design*, and
it too is L2's. **Stated correctly the fact is stronger, not weaker: every open or deferred design point
in the framework sits at L2, and none sits at L1.** The correction was put to the user with the surface.

**The ground, on the two axes Ruling 1 requires.** On the **objective** axis the two options do not
separate, and the fact that makes them not separate is **§5's ratified boundary contract**: what L1
hands L2 is fixed at charter level, and a detail specification derives *how*, not *what crosses*. If
L2's derivation later needs something L1's charter does not publish, that is an **amendment**, on an
identical route in either order. **So order changes WHEN we learn, not what the analysis can ultimately
achieve** — and if that fact were wrong, L2-first would win. On the **principles** axis L0+L1 rates
higher: it touches **no** open design point (the correction above), it runs on the territory the pass
most strongly corroborated with no falsifier in twenty rows, and it exercises the phase's never-run
method — derive first, witnesses read only after each derived statement is written — on bounded material
where a defect in the procedure is still distinguishable from a difficulty in the material. **#5 cuts
against L2-first here rather than for it**: three underived points sit in its path and the pass
established the reachable literature settles none of them, so an L2 derivation meets questions needing
a ruling or new research, not more derivation. **The axes therefore point the same way and Ruling 1's
precedence clause did not come into play** — recorded because Ruling 1 requires the question asked.

**★ THE ARGUMENT AGAINST, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE USER'S OWN AND WAS NOT DISMISSED.** From Ruling 2's
grounds: *it is the entire architecture as a whole that is evaluated against the ultimate objective, not
separate pieces individually — competing algorithms are coupled, and choosing A over B at one place can
enable a better C downstream.* It is a live argument for L2-first. What answers it is not dependency
order — which the pass weakened, L1 having drawn support and not moved — but the boundary-contract fact
above, which bounds the couplings that matter to the ratified charter rather than leaving them to the
detail specifications. **And the usual second argument for going at the hard piece first is spent: the
reading pass was commissioned to find the frame's unsupported places early and it found them.**

**A recommendation was given rather than withheld, and its own footing was declared.** The surface
stated plainly that **D-249** asks a surface for its recommendation while **D-658** would have it gather
facts and make none, that **which governs is §3's still-unruled question**, and that the user should
discount the recommendation if he preferred the facts bare. He did not.

**What it does NOT do. IT AUTHORIZES NO DERIVATION YET.** The eighty-seventh entry's gate stands
unchanged: no derivation may begin before the chosen subject's slice of **Task B** is in, and Task B's
reading has not begun. **L2 is SECOND, not deferred** — no design point of L2's is touched, closed or
prejudged, and the three underived ones stay open exactly as `FRAMEWORK.md` leaves them. It moves no
design point, no charter, no boundary contract and no phase. It touches no open-items row, no
decisions-register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool, no measured value and no code.

**Its one consequence for work already on disk, stated as a derivation and not as a ruling.** The
candidacy-upgrade derivation at `reading_pass/candidacy_upgrades.md` proposes a reading order headed by
L2's entangled decision, and that order is *"stated, not ruled"* in its own words. **This ruling makes
the phase's first needed slice L1's**, which is a consequence of the ordering decision rather than an
override of that file; the commission's §3 already provides for a bounded slice taken in the order the
work requires. **Which rows constitute that slice is this side's derivation, challengeable at its own
rows, and is not ruled here.**

**Execution.** **No dispatch is written here** (**D-250**), and nothing in this ruling is an edit to a
tracked document. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

*(Written under the disambiguation convention: the non-musical sense of the collided word is qualified
throughout this section. The bare uses standing elsewhere in this file are inherited and are the scoped
terminology pass's business — `OPEN_ITEMS.md` OI-229 — not this act's.)*

## 3k. Ruling 11 — THE L0+L1 BOOT PACK'S READING LIST, ON TWO OF ITS THREE DECISIONS (Decision 1: Option A; Decision 2: Option B)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, carrying three decisions, each option rated on the objective
axis and the principles axis as **Ruling 1** requires. **The user's words, verbatim: "Agree on your
recommendations so far."** **He disposed directly upon the surface, pre-empting the separate question
turn D-249 provides for; that is declared here, not dressed up** — the same declared shape as the
2026-08-30 opening sitting and the 2026-08-28 framework-delta sitting.

**★ HOW THE WORDS ARE READ, WRITTEN DOWN SO A ONE-WORD CORRECTION IS AVAILABLE.** *"So far"* is read as
**the recommendations the surface actually gave, and no more.** It gave one on Decision 1 and one on
Decision 2. **It gave NONE on Decision 3, which was stated as owed rather than proposed** — so
**Decision 3 IS NOT RULED and stays owed.** A ruling is not widened onto a decision the surface
declined to recommend on.

**DECISION 1 — HOW THE RATIFIED CHARTER ENTERS THE PACK. RULED: OPTION A.** The L0+L1 boot pack carries
**`FRAMEWORK.md` §5 and §9 ONLY, leak-filtered** by the mechanism the pilot's design-intent member
already uses, with **Appendix A and every passage describing what this project currently has removed**.
**Option C — the same members unfiltered, with the stop-on-meeting clause carrying the load — is the
RULED FALLBACK if the filter proves more than a small act.** **Option B — the whole framework document
— is DECLINED.**

*The ground, on the two axes.* On the **objective** axis A rates highest: a derivation bounded by the
charter and by nothing else states what L1 SHOULD do rather than commenting on what exists. B rates
worst and not marginally, on a fact checked at the file: **the framework document's Appendix A.4 is
headed *"Where the derived answer DIFFERS from what this project already has"*** — the most
concentrated account of the current implementation the repository holds, sitting inside the one
document the session must read; a session that reads it writes every later statement knowing the
answer, and the output would be a comparison wearing the form of a derivation. On the **principles**
axis A is strongest on the implementation-blind rule, which **§3.2 and §3.8 of
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_phase_definition_surface_2026_08_15.md` make a standing constraint over
every phase**, and on **#19**, the filter being a mechanism whose output is inspectable; C substitutes a
session's own discipline for that mechanism, which #19 declines — a session that met nothing and a
session that failed to notice leave the same record. **The two axes agreed on all three options, so
Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play**, and the question was asked because Ruling 1
requires it.

**DECISION 2 — WHETHER THIS SESSION'S READING-PASS EXTRACTS GO INTO THE PACK. RULED: OPTION B.** The
five L1-slice extracts go in, **with their *"What an L1 detail specification could adopt, adapt, or must
argue against"* sections CUT.**

*The ground.* On the **objective** axis the session meets five papers already read at the object —
which is the primary-read debt **Ruling 2 of 2026-08-30** meant to pre-pay in its own words — while
reaching its own conclusions over them rather than inheriting mine. On the **principles** axis #1 and #2
are served, the evidence being fetched-and-read literature with every claim labelled, and the phase's
bar against a deriving session inheriting an answer is kept. **The cost is one more filtering rule to
write and to verify.** *Recorded because the surface said it before the ruling and the record should
carry it:* **the argument for Option A was Ruling 2's own words and the argument for B was this side's
judgment about its own writing**, so this recommendation was offered as the weaker of the two, and the
user ruled it anyway. **The extracts are not implementation-derived** — they come from published
research — so neither option turned on blindness.

**DECISION 3 — ANNOTATED SCORES STAGED BY NAME. NOT RULED, AND NOT PROPOSED.** The surface declined to
put options on it, on the stated ground that choosing L1 exemplars means choosing from what this side
already knows about our own corpus, which is the wrong footing. **It stays owed**, and it must be
settled before the pack is rendered, since the pilot's brief stages such files **by name and never a
directory**, as **exemplars and not a corpus**, with the bar that the session builds, designs, scopes
and runs **no** measurement over them.

**What it does NOT do.** **It authorizes no derivation.** It renders no pack, writes no brief and boots
no session. It amends **no** part of `FRAMEWORK.md` — the filtering is of what a pack CARRIES, not of
what the document SAYS, and the document is untouched. It moves no design point, no charter and no
boundary contract. It touches no open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus, no measurement
tool and no code. **It does not settle Decision 3, and it does not settle the D-249/D-658 conflict**,
which §3d records as open and which this surface declared bears on Decision 2 specifically.

**What follows mechanically, none of it executed here.** The reading list is now ruled on its two
decidable points, so what is owed next is: **Decision 3's own surface**; then a **CC dispatch to render
the pack** — the leak filter of Decision 1 and the section cut of Decision 2 both being mechanisms it
must build and verify — and then **the brief for the blind deriving session**, which this side writes
and the user opens with a fresh Cowork session (the 2026-08-26 role ruling; this side never starts
them). **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3l. Ruling 12 — DECISION 3, THE ANNOTATED SCORES: PLAIN SCORES ONLY, SELECTED BY A CRITERION DECLARED BEFORE IT RUNS (Option B)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, four options each rated on the objective axis and the
principles axis as **Ruling 1** requires, every load-bearing claim checked at a file and cited there.
**The user's words, verbatim: "B it is."** He disposed directly upon the surface again; declared, not
dressed up.

**THE RULING. For the L0+L1 boot pack, PLAIN SCORES ONLY — no published analyses — selected by a
MECHANICAL criterion over the corpus's own note tables, the criterion written and shown to the user
BEFORE the selection is run.** Three conditions are part of the ruling rather than additions to it:
**(i)** the criterion is shown first and the selection is a mechanical act, never a judgment; **(ii)**
the pilot's two standing bars carry over unchanged — files staged **BY NAME and never a directory**,
and **EXEMPLARS, NOT A CORPUS**, with the session building, designing, scoping and running **no**
measurement over them; **(iii)** the gap is declared to the session rather than hidden — fermatas and
partial signatures are not selected for, so a session wanting such a case **writes the want as an open
question and does not fill it**, which is the pilot brief's own precedent for an undeclared hole.

**★ THE OVERTURN CONDITION, RULED WITH IT AND CHECKED FIRST.** If the criterion does not SEPARATE the
corpus — if release-driven change points are near-uniform across the chorales — **B degenerates into a
random pick and OPTION A (stage nothing) becomes the ruled outcome**, because staging three arbitrary
chorales would suggest an evidential basis the selection does not have. **This is checkable before
anything is staged and is the first step of building the criterion**, not a later review.

**The ground, on the two axes.** On the **objective** axis B rates highest: L1's charter turns on
notational reality — *"change points at every onset AND every release"*, ties, metric position — and the
charter's own release clause exists because a case of that kind corrected an earlier draft, so a session
with no notation before it derives from abstraction. The selection is computable because **the corpus
ships its own note tables** (`tools/dcml/bach_chorales/notes/*.notes.tsv`), carrying per note the onset
in quarter-beats, the **duration**, a **tied** column, voice and staff, metric position and time
signature — **so releases, and therefore change points that are not onsets, are mechanically derivable**
— beside `metadata.tsv`'s per-piece time signature, key signature, repeat bars and onset counts against
distinct onset positions. On the **principles** axis it is **#17(b)**'s shape applied to selection — the
criterion is written down before it is run, so the pick cannot be tuned to make an answer look right;
**#19** is served, the criterion's output being a re-runnable artefact; and **#5** is served, the
evidence being cheaply available. **The declared cost:** fermatas and partial signatures cannot be
selected for, the first being absent from both tables and the second requiring a signature judged
against a key, which needs an analysis.

**THE THREE DECLINED OPTIONS, recorded because an excluded alternative is evidence about the choice.**
**A — stage nothing:** clean on #19 and consistent with the pilot's precedent, but it declines cheap
evidence, against #5. **C — scores with their published analyses, pilot-style:** it adds an oracle that
answers a DIFFERENT LAYER's question — **DP-I puts cues at L1 and cadence TYPE at L3**, and a
Roman-numeral analysis says where an analyst heard a chord, never where the sounding set changes — and
one of the two annotation families is **not established as an instrument** (**D-475**: one named
annotator, no independent second annotation, on a reduction, through a machine translation), so it
invites treating an unestablished annotation as a standard, which is what #19 exists against. **D —
scores selected by the writing side reading them:** declined **by this side on its own account**, the
selecting party carrying knowledge of where the analysis currently struggles, which makes its neutrality
an unverifiable claim about itself — the class **Ruling 1(a)** requires be labelled a reading rather
than stated as a fact. **The two axes agreed on the ranking, so Ruling 1's precedence clause did not
come into play.**

**★ ONE ITEM IS NOT A RULING AND IS RECORDED AS A CORRECTION OF THIS SIDE'S OWN WORK.** The reading list
put to the user at Ruling 11 **omitted the empirical findings ledger**, which **§3.4 of
`ratification_surfaces/cowork_phase_definition_surface_2026_08_15.md` names among this phase's inputs in
terms** — *"the same independent sources and ledger as the framework phase."* `EMPIRICAL_FINDINGS_LEDGER.md`
therefore enters the pack **by the phase definition's own naming and not by any decision taken here**;
the omission was this side's and is stated rather than quietly repaired.

**What it does NOT do.** It selects no score, stages nothing, renders no pack, writes no brief and boots
no session. It authorizes no derivation and no measurement — the criterion's separation check is a
property of the corpus's own note tables and is **not** a measurement of this project's analysis. It
touches no open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus file, no measurement tool and no code.
**No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

**What follows mechanically, none of it executed here.** The three decidable points of the L0+L1 reading
list are now ruled, so what is owed is: **the criterion, written and shown to the user**; then **a CC
dispatch**, because the separation check and the selection run over the whole corpus's note tables and
**a shell on the repository is not this side's working method** (**D-253** and its 2026-08-08 widening;
the shell surface's answer is still owed) — the same dispatch carrying Decision 1's leak filter and
Decision 2's section cut, both mechanisms it must build and verify; and then **the brief for the blind
deriving session**, which this side writes and the user opens with a fresh Cowork session (the
2026-08-26 role ruling).

## 3m. Ruling 13 — THE L0+L1 BRIEF'S P1, P2 AND P4 (all three as recommended)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, three points each with its options rated on the objective axis
and the principles axis as **Ruling 1** requires. **The user's words, verbatim: "Agree on all three
recommendations."** He disposed directly upon the surface; declared, not dressed up.

**P1 — WHO DERIVES. RULED: A FRESH COWORK SESSION.** CC and a continuing session are both declined.

*The ground.* The pilot ruled the same for its own unit (Ruling 2 of
`cowork_rulings_2026_08_24_sizing_pilot_sitting.md`), and the user's own recorded words on the reading
pass point the same way — *"If necessary we should let a fresh LLM session do it."* **★ AND THE
CONTINUING SESSION IS DISQUALIFIED ON A CHECKABLE FACT RATHER THAN ON MODESTY: the ordinary
session-start read includes `CLAUDE.md` whole, and `CLAUDE.md`'s local-patches section describes in
detail how a key signature and a declared mode are read out of a MusicXML file — the `addKey()` dedup,
what happens to a zero-fifths signature carrying an explicit mode, and which stems lost their mode
anchor. That is implementation knowledge about how this project turns a notated record into note
events, which is L0's and L1's own subject.** So **any session that has performed the ordinary
session-start read is disqualified from deriving this subject**, and that includes the session that
wrote the brief. **CC was declined on a different ground and one that is NOT asserted either way:**
whether CC can fetch published research is **not established** in the record read for the surface, and
it was labelled unestablished rather than guessed (**Ruling 1(a)**); what decided it is that D-252's
split is between instruction-writing and the execution of repository acts, and a derivation is not the
latter.

**P2 — MAY THE DERIVING SESSION FETCH AND READ PUBLISHED RESEARCH. RULED: ALLOWED, UNBOUNDED.**

*The ground.* The pilot ruled it allowed on the ground that the method's ruled inputs are the pack **and
fetched literature**, so a session forbidden research exercises a different method from the one
established. **The phase definition settles it directly:** §3.4's inputs are *"the same independent
sources and ledger as the framework phase"*, and §3.3 names the framework phase's inputs as including
*"published research fetched and read"* — so forbidding research would put the session outside the
phase's own definition. **The bounded variant was declined on #19**: five papers bearing on L1 are read
at the object and their extracts go into the pack under Ruling 11 Decision 2, and barring the session
from those primaries would make **this side's own extracts unfalsifiable inside the session that most
depends on them** — an extract is a relayed reading, and a session forbidden the primary must trust it.
**What is ordered instead is a REPORTING bound, not a reading bound:** the session records which of the
five it re-read and why, so a later reader can tell a check from a duplication.

**P4 — THE OUTPUT. RULED: ONE FILE FOR BOTH CHARTERS, DATED —
`cowork_blind_derivation_l0_l1_2026_08_31.md`.**

*The ground, and the question that was hiding inside the naming.* **L0 and L1 are two charters, not
one**, and the phase's postcondition is *"every specification derived"* — per specification — so whether
this yields one derived text or two **is not settled by anything the surface's author had read**, and
that was stated rather than glossed. One file was taken because **L0's content is almost entirely
constraints on L1** — what may be assumed of the notated record, what happens when a real score is
deficient — so a split would put half of each statement in the other file, which #6 disfavours where two
concerns interlock this tightly. The date matches the pilot's own naming, which dates at the ruling
rather than at the run. **A condition is part of the ruling: if the deriving session finds the two
charters separate cleanly, IT SAYS SO IN ITS OUTPUT**, and the split becomes the later act's business
rather than a decision taken now on no evidence.

**On all three the two axes agreed, so Ruling 1's precedence clause did not come into play** — recorded
because Ruling 1 requires the question asked. **The record largely decided all three, so D-599 was
running and what remained was ratifying the derivation rather than picking.**

**What it does NOT do.** **It does not make the brief dispatchable.** Two of §8's five points remain
open: **(P3)** whether the deriving session should meet notation at all — Ruling 12 settled the
criterion and its outcome, and the drafting of the brief has since produced evidence bearing on the
wider question, which is put to the user separately — and **(P5)** the boot pack, which does not exist
and whose rendering needs Ruling 11's two mechanisms. It boots no session, renders no pack, stages no
score and derives nothing. It touches no open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus, no
measurement tool and no code. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot
accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3n. Ruling 14 — WHERE THE FILTERED CHARTER MEMBER LIVES: THE PACK DISPATCH RENDERS IT, THE RULED MEMBER LIST IS UNTOUCHED (Option C)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, three routes rated on the objective axis and the principles
axis. **The user's words, verbatim: "P5 - I agree with your recommendation."**

**THE RULING. The pack-rendering dispatch RENDERS a pre-filtered `FRAMEWORK.md` §5-and-§9 member and
VERIFIES it, without touching the generator's ruled member list.** The verification runs both ways: the
passages named below are absent from the rendered member, **and nothing else was removed**.

**The ground.** On the **objective** axis the session gets exactly what **Ruling 11 Decision 1** ruled —
§5 and §9 with the passages describing this project's own record removed — and the removal is **a
mechanism whose output is inspectable**, which is the property Decision 1 chose Option A for in the first
place. On the **principles** axis it leaves `MEMBERS` untouched, so **nothing the user ruled is amended
by a dispatch** and neither existing pack moves.

**★ THE FILTERING CONTENT, ESTABLISHED BY READING §5 AND §9 WHOLE AT THE FILE.** Two passages, both
about what this project's specifications say rather than what its code does: **DP-N's** *"Stage two
established that this project's own layer specifications do not state the answer either…"* and
**DP-Q's** *"Stage two found the same question open in this project's own record…"*. **Appendix A never
enters**, Decision 1 taking §5 and §9 only.

**THE TWO DECLINED ROUTES.** **Extending the generator** — the durable answer, and what a later subject
will eventually force — declined for now because `MEMBERS` is **global**: it carries the ruled six for
every subject alike and has no per-subject dimension, so adding members either changes what the two
already-rendered packs carry or must first gain that dimension, and the list was **ruled by the user on
2026-08-22**; a dispatch may not amend it. **Ruling 11's own fallback (Option C there: unfiltered, with
the stop-on-meeting clause carrying the load)** declined on Ruling 11's own reasoning, unchanged by the
two passages being few: **#19's objection stands that a session which met nothing and a session which
failed to notice leave the same record.**

**The overturn condition, carried from the surface.** If rendering a member outside the generator would
leave the pack's read-me describing a file list that does not match what the directory holds, **route C
creates a second inconsistency to maintain and extending the generator becomes right despite its cost**.
**That is checkable when the dispatch is written, and is to be checked BEFORE writing rather than
after.**

**What it does NOT do.** It renders nothing, writes no dispatch and boots no session. It amends no
governing document and no ruled artefact — in particular **`MEMBERS` and the two existing packs are
untouched**. It does not settle **(P3)**, which stays open. **No decisions-register identity is
allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3o. Ruling 15 — (P3) THE DERIVING SESSION MEETS NOTATION, AND THE SET SPANS NAMED PHENOMENA RATHER THAN REPRESENTING A CORPUS

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text by the session that booted on
