`cowork_handoff_entry_eighty_seven.md`, **after the user challenged this side's own recommendation of
the preceding turn**. **The user's challenge, verbatim: "disregarding that certain scores have 'ground
truth' we compare with - is then not more scores of different styles a good (and strong) thing towards
the ultimate objective?"** **His ruling, verbatim: "I agree with your recommendation."** — given upon
the revised surface the challenge produced.

**★ THE CHALLENGE WAS UPHELD, AND THIS SIDE'S EARLIER WEIGHING IS RECORDED AS WRONG RATHER THAN QUIETLY
REPLACED.** The preceding surface rated staging beyond the chorale corpus poorly on the principles axis,
on the ground that *choosing which scores is this side's hand again*. **That objection belongs to a
selection which claims to be REPRESENTATIVE. It does not belong to one which claims only that named
scores EXHIBIT NAMED PHENOMENA — because that claim is checkable at the score.** Two kinds of selection
were collapsed into one and the objection imported from the wrong one. **The objective-axis case was
also under-rated:** the faces the chorales cannot exercise are exactly the ones the charter leaves most
open — **face (a), what makes a note ELIGIBLE to open a change point, which is the largest hole in the
subject and is about grace notes, ornaments written as small notes, trills, tremolo and cue notes, none
of which four-part chorale homophony contains** — and the same texture is why the release-driven case
sits at about a hundredth of all change points there. **And the record already carried the argument
more strongly than the surface did: D-201 — *"Very large scores must be handled, and are expected to be
more common than our corpora."***

**THE RULING. The L0+L1 deriving session MEETS NOTATION, and the staged set is chosen to SPAN
NOTATIONAL PHENOMENA NAMED IN ADVANCE from the charter's own open faces**, each score's claim **checked
at the file before it is staged**. The phenomena, each tied to the face it serves: **ornaments, grace
notes, trills and tremolo** (face (a), eligibility); **staggered entries, so releases fall away from
onsets** (faces (a) and (c), and the release case at real density); **rests and genuine silence** (face
(c)); **a time-signature change** (face (d)); **fermatas, repeats and double bars** (face (e)); **more
than one kind of cadence** (face (f)); and **keyboard or orchestral voicing, where voice membership is
a notational convenience rather than a fact** (L0's contract and face (a)).

**The shape: about four scores** — one chorale, because it is the gate repertoire and the corpus's own
idiom; one contrapuntal movement for staggered entries; one keyboard piece for ornaments; and one
carrying a meter change if the tree holds one.

**The bars carry over unchanged from the pilot:** staged **BY NAME and never a directory**; **EXEMPLARS,
NOT A CORPUS**, with the session building, designing, scoping and running **no** measurement over them.

**Three constraints ruled with it, being what survived this side's objection.** **(i)** The set stays
**small** — a deriving session's context is finite and the answer is a spanning set, never a second
corpus. **(ii)** The set stays inside the **charter's scope**, common-practice tonal music as notated;
the jazz and tango material `docs/score_inventory.md` records is outside it and is not staged.
**(iii)** A **practical constraint is UNESTABLISHED and must be established before anything is
promised**: `tools/extra scores/` holds `.mscz`, a zipped binary container, while the chorales are
plain-XML `.mscx`. **If a `.mscz` cannot be read as staged, the set is constrained to `.mscx` material
and that is reported rather than worked around.**

**What it does NOT do.** **It stages nothing.** The next act is a READ, not a dispatch: establish at the
objects which in-tree scores carry which phenomenon, and whether a `.mscz` is readable as staged, then
bring the named set to the user with each score's claim beside it. **It does not disturb Ruling 12**,
which settled a criterion over the chorale corpus and its outcome; *which phenomena, from where* is a
question that ruling never reached. **It promotes nothing into the gate** — **D-308** is not engaged,
nothing is acquired, and the staged files are exemplars. It amends no governing document, touches no
open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool and no code. **No
decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3p. Ruling 16 — RULING 14's OVERTURN CONDITION FIRED; THE GENERATOR IS EXTENDED

**The occasion.** Ruling 14 chose route C — render the filtered charter member outside the generator —
and ruled its own overturn condition: *if that would leave the pack's read-me describing a file list
that does not match what the directory holds, extending the generator becomes right despite its cost*,
**to be checked BEFORE the dispatch is written**. **It was checked, at
`tools/audit/gen_derivation_boot_pack.py`, and it fired — in three places, not one.**

**THE THREE FINDINGS, each read at the file.** **(1)** `render_read_me` hard-codes the count in its own
prose — its heading is *"The six files of this pack, in order"* — and builds the list from `MEMBERS`, so
three members added outside would make the FIRST thing a deriving session reads state six over a
directory of nine. **(2)** The manifest records the same list at `the_pack_files_in_order`. **(3)
DECISIVE: `check_all` compares the directory listing against the generated set** — *"the directory
holds […], not […]"* — so **any file rendered into a pack directory outside the generator makes that
pack permanently STALE**. That is not an inconsistency to maintain; it is a standing red check.

**A FOURTH ROUTE WAS FOUND AND PUT TO THE USER RATHER THAN TAKEN SILENTLY.** The generator's own
read-me states the boundary: *"What you may read beyond this directory — your brief, score and analysis
files your brief stages to you by name, and published research — is stated by your brief, and by nothing
in this directory."* So the three additions could have been **staged by the brief, by name**, exactly as
the four scores are — leaving the read-me true, the manifest true, `--check` green and `MEMBERS`
untouched, **at the cost that nothing would re-derive them.**

**THE RULING. The user's words, verbatim: "extend generator".** **The generator gains a PER-SUBJECT
dimension of membership**, and the `l0-l1` pack carries the three additions **as members**, inside the
pack's own integrity check.

**The shape this must take, and it is what protects what the user already ruled.** `MEMBERS` is global
and **was ruled by the user on 2026-08-22**. The extension is therefore **ADDITIVE and PER-SUBJECT: the
ruled six stay in every pack, unchanged and in their ruled order, and a subject may carry EXTRAS after
them.** For the two existing subjects the extras list is **empty**, so **both existing packs and the
manifest must re-render BYTE-IDENTICAL** — and that is a prediction to be stated before the act and
proven after it, not an expectation to be checked casually. **The read-me's "six files" prose becomes
DERIVED from the actual member count**, which is a prose defect the extension itself causes and must
therefore fix.

**What supersedes and what does not.** **Ruling 14's choice of route C is SPENT** — its own overturn
condition fired and named the successor. **Its other half stands unchanged and is carried into this
ruling:** the filtered charter member is still **§5 and §9 with the two passages removed** — DP-N's
*"Stage two established that this project's own layer specifications do not state the answer either…"*
and DP-Q's *"Stage two found the same question open in this project's own record…"* — **verified both
ways: those absent, nothing else removed.** Ruling 11's Decisions 1 and 2 and Ruling 12's ledger
correction are untouched.

**What it does NOT do.** It renders nothing and writes no dispatch; it authorizes the dispatch to be
written. It removes no ruled member and reorders none. It does not change what any existing pack
carries — the byte-identity of both is a required proof, not a hope. It boots no session and derives
nothing. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3q. Ruling 17 — THE BOOT-PACK BATCH'S THREE OWED QUESTIONS, ONE RATIFICATION, AND THE STAGED SET AMENDED

**The occasion.** `cc_l0l1_boot_pack_2026_08_31` extended the generator, proved the extension additive,
and **STOPPED before the render** on a start-state condition. **The user's words, verbatim: "check
beethoven. I agree on recommendations too."**

**★ AND ONE ERROR OF THIS SIDE'S, COUNTED, BECAUSE IT IS WHY NOTHING RENDERED.** The dispatch's Task 6
ordered `--check` GREEN **and** both existing packs byte-identical. **From the actual start state those
cannot both hold**, and this side had the information to know it: **the eighty-seventh handoff entry
states the guard set at TEN failing, and this session relayed that to the user in its own first report**,
then wrote a green-check condition without establishing whether this check was among the ten. **This is
the second dispatch of this session whose bar broke on a start state it did not check** — the first
broke on this side's own uncommitted ruling record. **The pattern is this side's, not CC's**, and the
standing fix is: *a dispatch establishes the start state of every check it will assert on, before it
asserts on it.*

**(a) THE TWO EXISTING PACKS ARE NOT RE-RENDERED. THEY ARE FROZEN, AND `--check` RANGES OVER LIVE
SUBJECTS.** **The ground is a fact established at the artifact, not a preference:** the four stale files
are members (2) and (4) of both packs, and the drift is **addition-only** — the first lines a re-render
would add are the **P-1 ordinary-session-start-read clause** and the **P-2 standing dispatch clause**,
**both ratified 2026-08-29, after both packs were rendered and after both their sessions ran.** **So
re-rendering would make those packs claim their sessions read rules that did not yet exist** — a
falsification of the record of what two completed derivations were given, which **#12** reaches
directly, and the pilot is what established the method this whole phase rests on. **D-646 is the ruled
shape for exactly this:** *a generated record that must outlive its own writer is frozen at an
established snapshot, and the freeze is enforced by a hash STOP.* `harmony-boundary` and `scoring-model`
are pinned at their current blobs as SPENT, with that hash STOP; **nothing is re-rendered and nothing is
lost**, and `l0-l1` can go green on its own.

**(b) THE SWEEP WIDENS THE FILTER BY ONE CANDIDATE AND NO MORE.** **REMOVED: candidate 1**, §5's
*"This is adopted from this project's material"* — pure provenance, the same shape as the two ruled
anchors, and its removal costs the deriving session nothing. **NOT REMOVED: candidates 2 and 4** —
DP-N's two disagreeing analyses and DP-Q's three exemplar analyses. **They describe CORPUS MATERIAL, not
what this project's system does, so they do not breach implementation-blindness; and both are
load-bearing EVIDENCE for their design points, so removing them would take a ground with the
provenance.** **Candidate 3 is MOOT** — the two ledger references point at a document the pack now
carries whole.

**(c) THE FOUR RESIDUALS ARE REPAIRED, AND THE BAR THAT FORBADE IT IS RELAXED BY NAME.** The read-me's
*"including one of these six"* would state six over nine **in the first thing a blind session reads**;
the three manifest fields still describe the pre-extension tool. **Task 6's requirement that the manifest
change only by the added subject is RELAXED for this purpose and for no other** — stated as a ruling
rather than assumed, because that bar is what protected the existing subjects.

**(d) CC'S ONE DECLARED CHANGE BEYOND THE LETTER IS RATIFIED.** Deriving the what-was-cut section from
the counts was correct: the alternative would have told an `l0-l1` session *"Nothing has been withheld"*
while three of its nine members had material silently deleted. **It offered the alternative and took the
right one**, and existing subjects still render byte-identically.

**(e) THE STAGED SET IS AMENDED — CORELLI RETAINED, A BEETHOVEN QUARTET MOVEMENT ADDED.** **Corelli
keeps its slot on its measured result:** it delivers staggered entries plainly and twice, and its part
NAMES are mismatched to its staff contents — two bass-clef staves named *violn* — **which is a genuine
L0 phenomenon, a file whose voice membership is given wrongly.** **But it delivers the release case only
weakly**, so the slot it was chosen for is unfilled by it.

**The ground for adding Beethoven, established at the files this session and NOT taken from genre
expectation.** The discriminator is the **sounding share**, `all_notes_qb / (length_qb × staves)`, read
from each corpus's own `metadata.tsv`: **chorale 001 is at 1.00 — every voice sounds at every moment and
the file contains not one rest** — against **0.755 to 0.883** across the Op. 18 movements examined.
**That is why release-only change points sit near one per chorale: such a change point needs a moment
where something stops and nothing starts, and this repertoire almost never provides one.** At the object,
chorale 001 returns **zero** matches for rests, trills and grace notes; Op. 18 no. 2 movement 1 returns
**765**. **The bound is declared: six movements of the seventy-one were examined, and the claim is an
EXISTENCE claim — that this repertoire supplies rests and silence the chorales do not — never a
maximality claim about which movement supplies most.**

**★ AND A CORRECTION THIS SIDE MADE BEFORE REPORTING, RECORDED BECAUSE IT WAS NEARLY SHIPPED.** A first
search returned ZERO fermatas in chorale 001 and this side was one step from reporting that as fact.
Widening it found **twelve**: **fermatas are encoded as `<subtype>fermataAbove</subtype>`, not as a named
`<Fermata>` element.** **That is itself a fact the deriving session needs for face (e)** — the notated
boundary evidence L1 must read is carried as an articulation subtype, so anything looking for a named
element misses it. The earlier claim that chorales mark phrase ends with fermatas was labelled THEORY
rather than asserted, which is why the check was run at all.

**What it does NOT do.** It renders nothing and stages nothing. It re-renders neither existing pack — the
freeze is the opposite of a refresh. It widens the filter by **one** named candidate and no other, and
**`FRAMEWORK.md` is not amended**: the filtering governs what a pack carries, never what that document
says. It touches no open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool and no
code. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3r. Ruling 18 — RULING 8's `FRAMEWORK.md` CORRECTION GOES IN A NARROW BATCH OF ITS OWN, THE PRESERVED WORDING IS NOT FILTERED, AND §14.1 STAYS OWED

**The occasion.** The `l0-l1` pack was rendered and **verified at the object by the writing side**, not
at the report: ten files, the ruled six intact, the charter member carrying §5 and §9 only with all
three removal anchors absent and no Appendix A. **That verification found a blocker the report could
not have caught, because it is not the batch's doing.** The rendered charter member carries
`### 9.0 The prior question: what is a unit? — PUT TO THE USER, NOT SETTLED` and closes *"Stated as this
phase's first ratified finding, for the user to rule. It is not settled here."* — **read at the rendered
object.** **Ruling 8 settled that question a day earlier and its correction has never been executed**;
the pack faithfully renders a document that is stale against a ruling.

**Why it blocks rather than merely annoys.** §9.0 is the **prior question** — what a unit IS. A blind
deriving session reading that it is open, with three candidate readings and an invitation to the user to
rule, may treat the grain of a unit as undecided and shape every statement it writes accordingly. **It
is the single item in that pack most able to bend the whole derivation**, and toward a state the record
has already left.

**The user's words, verbatim: "OK: the narrow batch, preservation unfiltered, §14.1 left owed."**

**(a) A NARROW BATCH, NOT THE BIG ONE.** The correction is **not** folded into the batch carrying Ruling
6's and Ruling 9's findings-surface corrections and the rest of what Task A leaves owed. **The fact that
decides it, established at the file by the eighty-seventh entry and re-read here: `FRAMEWORK.md` is NOT
touched by Rulings 6 or 9 at all** — the row-5 parse-space claim appears nowhere in it. **So a narrow
batch takes the WHOLE of that document's owed corrections and creates no double-touch**, while the big
batch keeps the findings-surface work, which is a different document. Against the ultimate objective the
narrow batch unblocks the first derivation now and leaves the big batch's scope to the user's own timing
rather than making it the derivation's gate.

**The ruled edit is closed and nothing is to be designed** (Ruling 8's own execution clause): **§9.0's
heading and closing sentence**, and **§11's R-6**, under the filing convention's **branch two** — body
corrected, former wording preserved in place (**#12**) — and *"No other edit to `FRAMEWORK.md` is
authorized."* **R-6's other three items — DP-N, DP-O, DP-Q — stand exactly as they are**, as Ruling 8
states. §9.0's candidate readings, its evidence paragraph and its what-each-reading-would-change
paragraph are **outside the ruled edit and untouched**; an excluded alternative is evidence about the
choice.

**(b) THE PRESERVED FORMER WORDING IS NOT FILTERED OUT OF THE PACK.** Branch two preserves it in place,
so the pack member will carry the words *"PUT TO THE USER, NOT SETTLED"* and *"It is not settled here"*
verbatim, and the deriving session will read them. **Three grounds, and the choice was put to the user
with its risk named rather than taken quietly.** A preservation block is explicitly marked as
superseded, so it reads as history and not as an open question — which is what **#12** is for. **The
sweep's own established test already excludes the framework's amendment history from being a filter
candidate**, and the boot-pack batch's report shows two such blocks examined and passed on exactly that
ground. And a removal on a new ground would set a precedent for **hiding from deriving sessions that the
charter they derive under has been corrected**, which costs more across the phase than the small risk it
removes. **The risk is not asserted to be zero:** the session meets the former wording inside the prior
question itself, and **a fourth anchor remains available if the user later wants it cut.**

**(c) §14.1's THIRD INSTANCE OF THE MISSTATED METRIC-STRENGTH FIGURE STAYS OWED AND UNAUTHORIZED.**
Neither ruling reaches it and no batch may edit it. **It is not in the pack member** — §5 and §9 only —
so it does not reach the derivation. **Named so the narrow batch's scope is a deliberate choice rather
than an oversight.**

**What it does NOT do.** It authorizes no derivation and no design. It reopens no design point — DP-N,
DP-O and DP-Q stay underived. It changes no charter, no boundary contract and no member of the pack
beyond what re-rendering the corrected source produces. It does not touch the two frozen packs. It
touches no open-items row, no decisions-register entry, no corpus, no measurement tool and no code.
**No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3s. NOT A RULING — NOTATED FIGURED BASS AS ANALYZER INPUT IS DEFERRED, AND THE DERIVATION MAY NOT FORECLOSE IT

**The occasion.** The user asked where in the plan the analysis should be extended to take **notated
figured bass in the score** as input. **His words, verbatim: "Not now, but beware of it possibly being
allowed as input."** **That is a deferral with a caution attached, not a ruling on admissibility**, and
it is recorded as such.

**The state of the record, established at the files rather than recalled.** **`FRAMEWORK.md` §5's L0
given-list does not include figured bass** — it names spelled pitch, duration, voice membership, metric
position and bar, time signature, key signature, bar lines, repeats, fermatas, ties, pedal marks, and
whether each note sounds and is visible. **L3 PUBLISHES figured bass** as a read-off fact, which is the
opposite direction from the one asked about. **The reading pass found no producer for it anywhere in the
read set**, which is why the automatic figured-bass annotation paper was admitted as a candidacy upgrade
and sits unread in the L2/L3 slice.

**★ THE PRIOR QUESTION, WHICH IS THE USER'S AND IS NOT SETTLED BY ANYTHING READ.** The record bans
**user-written analytical content** as analyzer input — chord symbols, Roman numerals, function, cadence
and key annotations — **D-501** stating that a written chord symbol may be read *only* as a comparison or
ground-truth label; and it admits **structural metadata** — key signature, time signature, ties, pedal.
**Notated figured bass sits between those two classes and nothing in the record places it:** it is
notation a contemporary hand wrote into the source rather than a modern editor's analysis, but what it
states is harmonic content and not a structural fact. **The closest precedent argues for admitting it
WEAKLY rather than banning it:** the key signature is exactly this shape — a notated hint about harmony
that L0 gives, admitted **as a weak prior and never as a fact about the tonality**, with **D-056**'s
*notes always win*.

**★ THE OPERATIVE CONSEQUENCE OF THE CAUTION, AND IT IS WHY THIS ENTRY EXISTS RATHER THAN A NOTE ON A
LIST. A derivation that writes L0's contract as a CLOSED LIST would foreclose this by construction**, and
the amendment would then be an edit to a ratified list rather than a decision against a stated test.
**The L0+L1 brief is therefore amended to require the session to state the CRITERION by which a fact is
admitted to L0's given list, beside the list itself.** With a criterion on the record, admitting figured
bass later is a decision judged against a test the derivation itself supplies; without one it is an
argument about a list.

**The amendment discloses nothing.** It names no candidate fact, does not mention figured bass, and says
nothing of this project's record or of this exchange — **a blind session must reach the question from the
domain or not at all**, which is also what makes its answer worth having.

**Why the question is not settled now, stated because the alternative was available.** Settling it now
would reopen a CLOSED framework phase and a ratified §5 before the phase depending on them has produced a
single statement. **Left to the derivation, the question is met by a session with no stake in the answer,
reaching it from the domain** — and the brief already requires that what cannot be settled is written as
an open question and never filled with the most plausible reading.

**What this does NOT do.** It does not admit figured bass, does not ban it, and does not amend
`FRAMEWORK.md` §5 or any charter. It moves no design point. It does not settle the separate and still-open
question of the **Corelli score's staff 3**, which carries a published Roman-numeral analysis inside the
file that Ruling 15's *plain scores, no published analyses* condition did not anticipate.

## 3t. CORRECTION OF RECORD — RULING 18(c)'s LOCATOR WAS WRONG, AND THE ITEM MAY BE MIS-CLASSIFIED AS WELL

**Not a ruling. A correction of this side's own record, made at the file rather than argued.** The
correction batch reported that Ruling 18(c)'s locator diverges from the document, and **this side
verified it at the object rather than relaying it.**

**★ THE LOCATOR IS WRONG, ESTABLISHED AT `FRAMEWORK.md`.** §14.1's bullet for that source reads, whole:
*"**The metrical constraint on harmonic change**, counted rather than assumed."* — **no figure at all**,
so nothing there can be a misstated one. **The uncorrected numeric restatement stands instead inside
APPENDIX B**, in the first-stage draft's own L1 paragraph: *"Temperley counted harmonic change at 71.5%
of tactus beats against 2.4% of the lowest metrical level"*, carrying a `[FACT.]` label — which is
exactly the misstatement Ruling 2 of this record corrected in §5 on 2026-08-31.

***THE FORMER WORDING OF RULING 18(c) IS PRESERVED (#12):*** *"§14.1's THIRD INSTANCE OF THE MISSTATED
METRIC-STRENGTH FIGURE STAYS OWED AND UNAUTHORIZED."* **What is corrected is the LOCATOR and nothing
else:** the item stays **owed and unauthorized**, no batch may edit it, and **it is not in the pack
member** — §5 and §9 only — so it does not reach the derivation, exactly as 18(c) states.

**Where the wrong locator came from, stated rather than left to be inferred.** It is not this side's
invention: **`cowork_rulings_2026_08_30_detail_phase_opening_sitting.md` §4 carries the same locator**,
and Ruling 18(c) took it from there instead of establishing it at the file. **That is the
never-work-from-memory rule's own shape applied to a locator** — a citation carried forward without
being opened. **Correcting that other record is not this side's act and is not done here**; it is
recorded so the user can decide, and so a reader of either record meets the divergence.

**★ AND A SECOND FINDING, WHICH MAY MATTER MORE THAN THE LOCATOR: THE ITEM MAY BE MIS-CLASSIFIED, NOT
MERELY MIS-LOCATED.** **Appendix B's own heading reads *"APPENDIX B — The first-stage draft, whole and
unedited"***, and `A.6` is headed *"The sealed files"*. **A restatement inside a document that declares
itself unedited is that appendix working as intended, not a defect in it** — and correcting its body
would falsify the record of what the first stage actually said, which is the thing the appendix exists
to preserve. **So what is owed there may be nothing at all, or a caveat at the appendix's head — branch
ONE filing under D-674, a dated record re-bannered and never rewritten — rather than the body edit the
record has been treating as owed.**

**★ AND IT MERGES WITH THE CORRECTION BATCH'S OTHER FINDING INTO ONE QUESTION, NOT TWO.** That batch
enumerated **five further passages of `FRAMEWORK.md` stating that §9.0 is unsettled, and reported all
five as untouched under Ruling 8's closed scope** — two in **Appendix A**, the stage-two amendment
record, and two more in **Appendix B** including that draft's own DP1, with a sixth textual match
recording a different question and unrelated. It left unadjudicated *whether a dated appendix's
present-tense sentence about a now-settled question is branch-one filing or something owed.* **That is
the same question this correction raises**, and **D-674** is the rule that governs both.

**Neither reaches the derivation, and that was checked rather than assumed:** the pack's charter member
carries **§5 and §9 only**, established at the tool's own authored spans, so Appendices A and B are
outside it by construction. **The question is therefore OWED and NOT BLOCKING**, and it is put to the
user as one decision rather than three findings.

**What this does NOT do.** It authorizes no edit to `FRAMEWORK.md` — Ruling 8's scope stays closed and
Appendix B stays unauthorized. It amends no other ruling record. It moves no design point, no charter
and no pack member. It does not settle the branch-one question it names.

## 3u. NOT A RULING — A SCORE FILE IS NOT NECESSARILY A NOTATIONAL RECORD, THE CORELLI QUESTION IS WITHDRAWN, AND THE PRE-STAGING CHECK GAINS A SECOND QUESTION

**Not a ruling. A finding of fact established at the score files, and the withdrawal of a question this
side put to the user twice on a false premise.** The user asked *"What is the problem with analysis in
the score — do we not trust it?"* — and the honest answer, found by going to the file instead of
answering from the framing, is that **trust was never the axis and this side's framing was wrong.**

**★ THE FACTS, ESTABLISHED AT THE OBJECTS, WITH THE COUNTS THIS SIDE READ.** Three DCML sub-repositories
were opened with file tools and they are **three different shapes**:

- **`bach_chorales/MS3/001 Aus meines Herzens Grunde.mscx`** — **2 staves, 0 `<Harmony>`, 0
  `<FiguredBass>`.** A plain notational record; that corpus's annotations live only in `harmonies/`.
- **`corelli/MS3/` — five files sampled across op. 1, op. 3 and op. 4** (`op01n08a`, `op01n01a`,
  `op03n01a`, `op04n01a`, `op04n06a`): **`<Harmony>` 56 / 64 / 78 / 74 / 22** and **`<FiguredBass>` 47 /
  42 / 71 / 50 / 21** — **every one carries both**, so the shape is the edition's convention and not one
  movement's accident. In `op01n08a` (4 staves) all 56 Roman numerals sit on **staff 3** and all 47
  figured-bass items on **staff 4**, and **staves 3 and 4 carry the SAME notes**: measure 1, four
  consecutive positions, pitches 60 / 56 / 55 / 43 with identical stem directions in both. **One bass
  line, written twice.** The part list names them organ, cello, *violn*, *violn*, with `instrumentId`
  `keyboard.piano` on staves 2, 3 and 4 — **the part naming is not reliable either.**
- **`bach_en_fr_suites/MS3/BWV806_01_Prelude.mscx` and `BWV806_10_Gigue.mscx`** — **2 staves, 0
  `<FiguredBass>`, but 191 and 122 `<Harmony>`**: Roman numerals **inline on the real music staves**,
  with no duplicated staff. A third shape again.

**★ SO THERE ARE THREE DISTINCT OBJECTIONS TO THE CORELLI FILE, AND ONLY THE THIRD IS ABOUT TRUST.**

**(a) The duplicated bass is an INPUT-CONTRACT defect, and it is the fatal one.** L1 is chartered on the
sounding set and where it changes — onsets, releases, ties, metric position. A file carrying the
continuo bass on two staves presents a **doubled bass at every one of those points**. That is not a
wrong analysis; it is a wrong *score*, and it corrupts precisely the facts this subject is about.
**Barring staff 3 does not repair it — staff 4 is the other half of the duplicate.**

**(b) The Roman numerals answer a DIFFERENT LAYER's question. That is relevance, not trust.** Ruling 12
already established it in declining its own option C: **DP-I puts cadence cues at L1 and cadence TYPE at
L3**, and a Roman-numeral analysis says **where an analyst heard a chord, never where the sounding set
changes.** A flawless analysis would still not answer face (a) or face (c).

**(c) The trust objection is real but narrow, and this side declines to overstate it.** **D-475** is on
the register — but it is about the **BCMH chorale** annotations, **not** the DCML Corelli set: one named
annotator, no independent second annotation, on a reduction, through a machine translation. **Nothing of
that kind is established about the DCML Corelli annotations and D-475 is NOT imported to them.** What
binds is **#19**: an instrument is not used until established. So the accurate statement is that their
establishment status is **unknown to this side**, that **unknown is not the same as untrusted**, and
that **it does not need settling, because (a) and (b) already dispose of the file.**

***THE FORMER FRAMING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"Three ways out: a different Baroque score for the
staggered-entries slot; keep Corelli and tell the session staff 3 is out of bounds; or accept it on the
ground that a published human analysis is not our implementation."* **That option set is WITHDRAWN as
built on a false premise** — it treated the file's defect as an analysis to be trusted or not, when the
established defect is a duplicated bass that no bounds-instruction repairs. **The question is not put to
the user again in that form.**

**★ AND THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE PLAN, WHICH IS LARGER THAN THE ONE FILE.** **Ruling 12 ruled *plain
scores only*, and Ruling 15 ruled that each score's claim is *checked at the file before it is staged* —
but the claim Ruling 15 named is the PHENOMENON.** Both were written on an unstated premise: **that a
score file is a notational record.** Established false, in three different ways, in three sub-repositories
of one corpus family. **So the pre-staging check gains a SECOND question — *is this file a plain
notational record?* — with three parts: are annotation elements present; are any staves duplicated; is
the part naming what it claims.** And it is answered **PER FILE, never per corpus** — the chorales are the
exception here, not the rule. **This is stated as a finding for the user, not applied as an amendment by
this side.**

**Recorded because it explains the candidacy and bars a repair.** **`corelli/MS3/op01n08a.mscx` is one of
the ELEVEN pipeline snapshot sources** named in `docs/score_inventory.md`, whose **Hard rule 2** forbids
adding, removing or re-pinning them. That is why the file was in front of this side at all, and it is a
further reason the file is not edited into a plain copy.

**★ THE CANDIDATE SEARCH, AS FAR AS IT IS ESTABLISHED, AND WHERE IT STOPS.** `docs/score_inventory.md` was
read whole. **`tools/extra scores/` at top level is OUT OF SCOPE and is not searched further** — its
contents are jazz and pop (Bill Evans, Monk, Jobim, Snarky Puppy, Pentatonix), and **Ruling 15's
constraint (ii)** puts that material outside the charter's common-practice scope in terms.
**`tools/extra scores/large/` IS in scope**, and its registry rows carry measured counts: **`bach-the-art-
of-the-fugue.mscz`** is the natural staggered-entries exemplar but is **2303 measures / 62694 notes**,
which **Ruling 15's constraint (i)** — *the set stays small* — excludes; **`bach-brandenburg-concerto-no-4-
bwv-1049-mvt-iii-presto.mscz`** is **9 staves / 244 measures / 6361 notes, CC0, `ground_truth: false`**, a
fugal movement that would serve **staggered entries AND orchestral voicing in one file**, and is the
leading candidate. **Its `ground_truth: false` is the registry's claim and NOT a plainness check** — under
the finding above it is checked at the file before anything is promised.

**★ WHICH MAKES RULING 15's CONSTRAINT (iii) LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN INCIDENTAL, AND IT IS THE NEXT
ACT.** That constraint states the `.mscz` question is **UNESTABLISHED and must be established before
anything is promised**. **Every file in `large/` is `.mscz`, a ZIP container**, so the leading candidate
cannot be read by this side's file tools at all. The registry's measured staves, parts, measures and note
counts establish that **the project's own tooling reads them**; they establish **nothing** about whether a
session reading with file tools can. **Establishing it needs a shell on the repository, which D-253 makes
a CC dispatch and not this side's act** — the same route Ruling 12's own closing paragraph names.

**What this does NOT do.** **It stages nothing and selects nothing.** It amends **no** ruling — Rulings 12
and 15 stand exactly as written, and the second check question is put to the user rather than applied.
It edits no score, and it authorizes no stripped, decoded or otherwise derived copy of any score. It
touches no snapshot source and no pin. It moves no design point, no charter and no pack member, and it
reaches no pack file — nothing here is in member (7)'s §5-and-§9 span. It writes no dispatch. **No
decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3v. NOT A RULING — CONSTRAINT (iii) IS ESTABLISHED, THE DISPATCH'S OWN TEST RESTED ON A FALSE PREMISE, AND A THIRD CHECK QUESTION IS FOUND: WHOSE FILE IS IT?

**Not a ruling. The establishment Ruling 15's constraint (iii) ordered, now returned** — by
`cc_mscz_container_establishment_report.md`, **read whole by this side and its load-bearing claims
re-established at the objects rather than relayed.**

**★ WHAT THIS SIDE VERIFIED WITH FILE TOOLS, AND IT MATCHES THE REPORT EXACTLY.** At
`tools/dcml/ABC/MS3/n01op18-1_01.mscx`: `<museScore version="3.02">`, `<programVersion>3.6.2</programVersion>`,
`<programRevision>3224f34</programRevision>`, `<metaTag name="mscVersion">3.02</metaTag>`, and
**`<Harmony>` = 405** — the same 405 the report counts in the archive's inner file. **The report is
accurate where this side can check it**, which is the only ground on which its uncheckable parts are
carried forward at all.

**★ CONSTRAINT (iii) IS ESTABLISHED — BUT NOT BY THE TEST THIS SIDE DESIGNED FOR IT.** **A `.mscz` is a
lossless byte carrier of the `.mscx` it holds**, established positively on all three archives: every
member's stored CRC-32 verifies against its decompressed bytes, and each extracted `.mscx`'s byte count
equals the length its archive records. **That is #19's shape — positively established, not merely left
unfalsified.**

***AND THE TEST THIS SIDE DESIGNED TO ANSWER IT WAS BUILT ON A FALSE PREMISE, WHICH IS THIS SIDE'S
ERROR.*** The dispatch's §3(a) put `n01op18-1_01.mscz` beside `n01op18-1_01.mscx` on the ground that the
pair would isolate what a container costs. **They are not a zipped copy and its original: they are two
different MuseScore FORMAT VERSIONS** — `3.02` / `3.6.2` on disk against `5.00` / `5.0.0` inside the
archive. **The pair measures a format migration, and cannot separate that from the container.** The
batch surfaced this rather than building around it (#13). ***THE FORMER WORDING, PRESERVED (#12):***
*"Compare the archive's inner `.mscx` against that sibling … that last distinction is the load-bearing
one — it is what decides whether decoding a container recovers the notation or alters it."* **It decided
nothing of the kind, because the two objects do not stand in that relation.**

**What the failed test nevertheless yielded, and it is not nothing.** Over a **complete enumeration of
all 16,157 changed lines that reconciles arithmetically** — not a sample — **no `<pitch>`, `<tpc>`,
`<durationType>`, `<voice>`, `<sigN>` or `<sigD>` line differs.** Across a two-major-version migration
of this score, **no pitch, spelling, duration or time signature moved.** That is a fact about format
migration, **not** about zipping, and it is filed as such.

**★ AND THIS SIDE'S PREDICTION P-ii CARRIED A FALSE GROUND, RECORDED AS FALSIFIED.** The dispatch
predicted non-identity *"because the two mtimes are about a year apart."* **They are TEN DAYS apart** —
2026-04-05 and 2026-04-15. The claim held; **its stated reason was wrong**, and it was wrong because
this side read two thirteen-digit epoch-millisecond values off a directory listing and estimated their
difference instead of taking it. **The real ground was one the prediction did not name at all: the two
files are different format versions.**

***THE PATTERN IS NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT AS THREE INCIDENTS.*** In three consecutive entries this side
has asserted a **checkable** fact without checking it: **§3t**, a locator carried from another record
unopened; **§3u**, the framing that a score file is a notational record; and **here**, an arithmetic
difference estimated by eye. **All three are principle #18's class — checkable, unchecked,
load-bearing — and all three were caught downstream rather than at the point of assertion.** Stated so
the user can weigh whether this side's own checking discipline needs a bound, which is his to decide and
is not proposed here.

**★ THE FINDING NEITHER THE DISPATCH NOR THE BATCH WENT LOOKING FOR, AND IT MAY MATTER MORE THAN THE
ESTABLISHMENT.** The batch reported, as a labelled observation, that the archive's inner file carries
`<programRevision>abc123456</programRevision>` — *"a placeholder rather than a revision hash. What
produced that file is not established here."* **This side then read `version.cmake` at the object:**

> `set(MUSE_APP_VERSION_MAJOR "5")` · `set(MUSE_APP_VERSION_MINOR "0")` · `set(MUSE_APP_VERSION_PATCH "0")`
> · `set(MUSESCORE_REVISION "" CACHE STRING "Build revision")` · `set(MUSE_APP_UNSTABLE ON)`

**THIS REPOSITORY BUILDS VERSION 5.0.0, AND THE ARCHIVE'S INNER FILE DECLARES `programVersion 5.0.0`
WITH A PLACEHOLDER REVISION.** **The `.mscz` is dated ten days after its sibling — ten days after the
clone landed.**

**★ WHAT IS ESTABLISHED AND WHAT IS NOT, KEPT APART BECAUSE #18 IS EXACTLY THE PRINCIPLE IN PLAY.**
**Established:** the repository's declared version, the archive's declared version and revision, and
the ten-day gap. **NOT established:** that this build produced the file. **Two explanations fit and
neither is checked here** — a DCML score opened in this fork's own build and re-saved; or a synthetic
or test artifact, which `abc123456` also fits, being a deliberate placeholder rather than the empty
string an unset `MUSESCORE_REVISION` would give. **No causal claim is made and the question is not
answered by preferring the more interesting explanation.**

**★ BUT BOTH EXPLANATIONS SHARE THE CONSEQUENCE, AND THAT IS WHAT REACHES THE PLAN.** On either, the
file is **NOT a published third-party edition of that score.** And **a score written by this project's
own build, put in front of an implementation-blind session, is the very leak the blind-derivation
apparatus exists to prevent** — the session would be reading the music as this implementation renders
it. **This is stated as the reason the check is needed, not as a claim that the leak occurred: nothing
has been staged.**

**★ SO THE PRE-STAGING CHECK GAINS A THIRD QUESTION, AND IT IS THE CHEAPEST OF THE THREE. *WHOSE FILE
IS IT?*** `<museScore version>`, `<programVersion>` and `<programRevision>` are **three lines at the head
of any `.mscx`**, and they answer it. Beside §3u's two — *is it plain*, and *are staves duplicated* —
the pre-staging check is now three questions, all answerable in one read of a file's first few hundred
bytes plus three counts. **Stated as a finding for the user, not applied as an amendment by this side.**

**★ AND THE SAME CHECK EXONERATES THE LEADING CANDIDATE, WHICH IS A POSITIVE FINDING AND IS REPORTED AS
ONE.** The batch recorded each extracted file's declared version. **Brandenburg's inner `.mscx` is
museScore `3.01` / `programVersion 3.0.0`; Haydn's is `2.06` / `2.3.2`.** **Both predate this fork's
5.0.0 by two and three major versions, so NEITHER can be this build's output** — and both archives'
registry rows carry a musescore.com `source` URL and a CC0 licence read from the file's own copyright
metaTag. **Their provenance is third-party, on the same evidence that makes the ABC archive's
questionable.**

**★ AND THE BRANDENBURG CANDIDATE'S PLAINNESS, WHICH IS THE BEST RESULT IN THIS ENTRY.**
`bach-brandenburg-concerto-no-4-bwv-1049-mvt-iii-presto.mscz` → `temp_10111.mscx`: **`<Harmony>` 0,
`<FiguredBass>` 0, `<StaffText>` 0** — clean of all three annotation kinds, where the prediction reached
only the first two; **9 part-list and 9 body staves**, matching the registry's measured row; nine
`<instrumentId>` values that are the concerto-grosso scoring.

**★ THREE STAFF PAIRS MATCH OVER THE TESTED WINDOW, AND THE READING OF THAT IS THE OPPOSITE OF
CORELLI'S — LABELLED A READING, NOT STATED AS A FACT (Ruling 1(a)).** The pairs are **1↔4 Violino
principale / Violino 1**, **2↔3 the two recorders**, and **7↔9 Violoncello / Continuo**. **This side
reads those as real doublings of the concerto-grosso scoring — music a listener hears — and NOT as
Corelli's case, which is one bass line written twice so that two annotation layers can hang off it.**
**The distinction is load-bearing for L0**: a doubled continuo is a genuine sounding fact the input
contract must carry, while Corelli's pair is an editorial artifact that corrupts it. **The reading is
checkable at the score and is NOT promised**: the batch tested 24 chords and reported *matches over the
tested window*, never *duplicate*, exactly as ordered — and the wider check is owed before the file is
staged. **That the ABC pair separated at chord fifteen, having been in unison for fourteen, is the
evidence that a 24-chord window can separate real parts and is why a wider one is owed rather than
assumed unnecessary.**

**Two open items the batch declined to guess at, carried here rather than dropped.** The Haydn file
carries **26 `<trackName>` and 14 `<instrumentId>` for 12 staves** — something names more instruments
than there are staves, and what is not established. And **`tools/ziprw/` is established from its own
sources as a ZIP read/write utility built on this project's own `muse::ZipReader` / `ZipWriter`** — on
the subject of this batch rather than an unrelated build dependency; not built, not run, and nothing
here depends on it.

**One procedural fact worth the record.** The batch's `git status --porcelain` was **refused by a
standing shell-read guard which named D-253 and its sanctioned substitute**,
`tools/audit/changed_paths.py`. **The guard chose the route, not the batch's judgment.** That such a
guard exists and fires is evidence about D-253's enforcement that this side did not previously hold.

**What this does NOT do.** **It stages nothing and selects nothing.** It amends **no** ruling — Rulings
12 and 15 stand as written, and the third check question is put to the user rather than applied. It
settles **nothing** about how a `.mscz`'s contents may reach a session: **that decision — hunt, decode,
strip, or bar-in-brief — is the user's and is not taken here.** It makes no causal claim about what
produced the ABC archive, and orders no investigation of it. It edits no score and authorizes no
decoded, stripped or derived copy of any score. It touches no snapshot source and no pin, no design
point, no charter and no pack member. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register
cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3w. Ruling 19 — THE STAGGERED-ENTRIES SLOT IS FILLED BY CONTAINER DECODE, WITH TWO CONDITIONS AND A DECLARED FALLBACK (Option B)

**The surface:** delivered as conversational text under **Ruling 1**, five options each rated on the
**objective** axis and the **principles** axis, every load-bearing claim read at a file and cited there.
**The user's words, verbatim: "Ok, go with: decode by dispatch — with two conditions that are part of it
rather than additions, and D as the declared fallback."** He disposed directly upon the surface.

**THE RULING. The staggered-entries slot is filled by DECODING `bach-brandenburg-concerto-no-4-bwv-1049-
mvt-iii-presto.mscz` BY DISPATCH and staging the `.mscx` the container holds.** The two conditions are
**part of the ruling, not additions to it**:

- **(i) THE WHOLE-STAFF CHECK RUNS BEFORE THE FILE IS PROMISED.** The three pairs matching over the
  tested 24-chord window — **1↔4 Violino principale / Violino 1**, **2↔3 the two recorders**, **7↔9
  Violoncello / Continuo** — are checked over **every chord of every staff**, not a window. **If a pair
  matches over the WHOLE staff, the file is in Corelli's class and the fallback fires**; if the pairs
  separate, they are concerto-grosso doublings and the file stands. *This side's reading that they are
  real doublings is a READING and is not promised (Ruling 1(a)).*
- **(ii) THE EXTRACTED FILE CARRIES A DECLARED NAME AND A PROVENANCE NOTE.** The container's inner member
  is named **`temp_10111.mscx`**, which names nothing about the music. The staged file is given a name
  that does, and carries beside it: the source `.mscz` and its `sha256`, that it arrived by container
  extraction, and **that no byte was altered** — proven by the extracted length equalling the archive's
  recorded length. **Naming is this side's hand and is declared as such rather than hidden.**

**THE DECLARED FALLBACK, ruled now rather than improvised later.** If condition (i) fails, the slot goes
to **Option D**: `tools/dcml/frescobaldi_fiori_musicali/MS3/12.15_Recercar_dopo_il_Credo.mscx` — 2 staves,
**162 `<Harmony>`**, 0 `<FiguredBass>`, genuine `programVersion 3.6.2` — **staged as published with
`<Harmony>` barred BY ELEMENT NAME in the brief**, and the bar's compliance recorded in the session's
independence record.

**The ground, on the two axes.** On the **objective** axis B rates highest because it is **the only
option that delivers a file with zero annotation of any kind — uncontaminated by construction rather
than by instruction** (`<Harmony>` 0, `<FiguredBass>` 0, `<StaffText>` 0), and because it serves **two**
named phenomena with one small file: staggered entries, and orchestral voicing where voice membership is
a notational convenience (L0's contract, face (a)). On the **principles** axis: **#19** is served, the
container decode being **positively established** — CRC verified on every member and extracted length
equal to recorded length across three archives — rather than assumed; **#12** is served, extraction
removing nothing; and the act is a container decode, **not an edit of a score**. **D-253** makes it a
dispatch and not this side's act.

**THE FOUR DECLINED OPTIONS, recorded because an excluded alternative is evidence about the choice.**
**A — keep hunting in-tree:** weak on objective, **five of five non-chorale DCML corpora checked carry
annotation in the score file** (corelli, ABC, bach_en_fr_suites, frescobaldi; chorales alone are plain),
and the plain material that does exist is either the wrong texture or whole works an order of magnitude
too large — `corpora/plain/StringQuartets` ships whole quartets as single `.mscx`, two sampled at 8.05
and 8.09 MB, excluded by Ruling 15's constraint (i), as `bach-the-art-of-the-fugue.mscz` is at 2,303
measures. **C — strip the annotation elements:** the only option with a principle **against** it —
removing elements from a score is information loss (**#12**) with no in-place preservation available in
a score file, and it imports **Ruling 12's own declined option D**, the selecting party editing the
material making its neutrality an unverifiable claim about itself. **D — bar in brief:** good and now the
ruled fallback; its named cost is that the brief's **stop-on-meeting clause exists because "do not read
that" is unreliable** — it makes a session record how much it saw before stopping — so D promotes a
backstop into a primary control, against elements met continuously rather than once. **E — declare the
hole:** **strongest on principles**, following Ruling 15's constraint (iii) verbatim and Ruling 12's
condition (iii) precedent, but **weak on objective**, leaving the derivation thinnest exactly where the
charter is most open. **★ E IS THE ONE PLACE THE TWO AXES CONFLICTED, AND RULING 1(c) DECIDED IT:** the
ultimate objective takes precedence.

**★ THE DISTINCTION THE WHOLE SURFACE TURNED ON, RECORDED BECAUSE IT WILL BE NEEDED AGAIN.** The session
reads a score as **XML text, not as engraved music**. `<Harmony>` is a distinctly named element in the
voice stream, so a session can be told to skip it **by name** and can record that it did — which is what
makes D possible at all. **A duplicated staff cannot be handled that way, because it looks exactly like
a real staff.** That is why Corelli is unfixable by instruction and an inline-annotated file is not.

**What it does NOT do.** **It stages nothing** — the decode is a dispatch and the staging is a later act.
It selects no other score and does not settle the remaining slots. It amends no ruling: Rulings 12 and
15 stand, and §3u's and §3v's added check questions remain findings put to the user rather than applied.
It edits no score and authorizes no stripped or derived copy. It touches no snapshot source and no pin,
no design point, no charter and no pack member. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that
register cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3x. NOT A RULING — THE USER'S QUESTION ON SCORE METADATA, ANSWERED WITH FACTS AND DEFERRED WITH A REASON

**The user's question, verbatim: "is it worthwhile to update our inventory-list of scores with meta-data;
the kind of 'tags' we need when looking for specific 'things' in scores?"** **Recorded so it survives
this sitting; nothing here is executed and no instrument is built.**

**What the tree already holds, read at the objects.** `docs/score_inventory.md` is a **usage map** in
prose — *which folder to reach for* — and is a live governing surface. `tools/score_census_registry.json`
carries **per-SOURCE** metadata (gt_type, license_class, distribution, tier, split, pinned_commit).
`tools/extra_scores_registry.json` carries **per-SCORE** rows with **measured** counts (staves, parts,
measures, notes, events, fileBytes) plus licence and source. `tools/corpus_registry.json` carries
per-run/per-score metadata, `tools/snapshot_sources_manifest.json` the sha256 and clone pins, and each
DCML corpus its own `metadata.tsv` and `notes/*.notes.tsv`. **There is no shortage of registries; there
are five.**

**What NONE of them records, which is the actual gap this sitting found.** Not one carries **the three
pre-staging check questions** — *is the file plain* (§3u), *are staves duplicated* (§3u), *whose build
wrote it* (§3v) — and not one carries **which notational phenomena a score contains**.

**The measured cost of not having it, from this sitting alone.** Corelli `op01n08a` was about to be
staged and was caught only by opening the file. Ruling 12's mechanical criterion **degenerated** and its
overturn condition fired, spending a whole CC batch. The fermata check on chorale 001 first returned
**0** on too narrow a pattern and found **12** on a wider one. And establishing the five-corpus
annotation pattern above took five separate stage-and-read passes that one generated table would have
answered in one read.

**★ THE LINE THAT MATTERS MOST, AND IT IS A PRINCIPLES LINE: TAG WHAT IS COUNTABLE, NEVER WHAT IS
JUDGED.** *"Has staggered entries"* is a **judgment**; selecting by it is selecting by this side's own
reading with a table around it — **Ruling 12's declined option D, laundered.** *`<Harmony>` = 0*,
*`<FiguredBass>` = 47*, *body staves = 9*, *`programVersion` = 3.0.0*, *`ornamentTrill` = 14*, *grace
notes = 0*, *time-signature changes = 2*, *fermatas = 12* are **mechanically countable**, re-derivable
and challengeable. **Ruling 12's own condition (i) already states the principle: the criterion is a
mechanical act, never a judgment.** Two further bounds follow: **#6**, this goes INTO an existing
registry or one generated artifact, never a sixth hand-maintained list; and **#19**, it is **generated by
a committed script and re-derivable**, because a hand-maintained table drifts and then gets trusted,
which is what #19 exists against.

**★ AND THE STRONGEST ARGUMENT FOR IT IS NOT SEARCH CONVENIENCE.** A per-score count of notational
phenomena is a **coverage map of the corpora against the charter's open faces** — it says what our
corpora **cannot** exercise. That is a fact the **measurement-design phase** needs, and Ruling 15 already
had to reason about it by hand (*"the faces the chorales cannot exercise are exactly the ones the charter
leaves most open"*).

**This side's answer, put as a recommendation and not taken: YES, but in two pieces at two times.**
**Now, and narrow:** the three check questions, applied to candidate files only — small, needed for the
ruled dispatch anyway, and no new instrument. **Later, at measurement design (phase 5):** the full
generated phenomenon-coverage map, because that is the phase whose work it serves and building it now is
scope creep across a phase boundary. **Neither is built here, and it blocks nothing** — Ruling 19's
dispatch does not need the table.

## 3y. RULING 19 IS EXECUTED — BOTH CONDITIONS MET — AND §3v CARRIED A FOURTH UNCHECKED CLAIM

**Not a ruling. The return of Ruling 19's dispatch, and a correction of this side's own record.**
`cc_exemplar_decode_report_2026_09_01.md` read whole; its checkable claims re-established at the
objects by this side.

**★ CONDITION (i) IS MET, AND IT WAS NOT A FORMALITY.** All 36 staff pairs diverge over the whole
staff; none is identical. The three pairs the 24-chord window had flagged **first diverge at chord

those indices lies beyond the tested window, and the last beyond it more than sixteenfold — so no
window shorter than 402 chords would have separated the Violoncello/Continuo pair.** Had the window's
result been read as a verdict, the file would have been judged Corelli's class, **the fallback would
have fired, and a clean exemplar would have been discarded.** The per-staff chord counts reconcile
against the file's own totals (6,361 = the `<Chord>` count = the `<pitch>` count), so the verdict is
about every chord and not a sample (DT-26).

**The prediction HELD in all three parts, and its GROUND was correctly NOT upgraded.** The dispatch
said *"the reading is this side's and is not evidence; the sequences are"*, and the batch held that
line: what is established is that the pairs separate, **not** that the agreeing stretches are
concerto-grosso doublings. **That reading remains this side's and remains unestablished.**

**★ CONDITION (ii) IS MET.** The copyright metaTag reads `OpenScore (CC0)`, checked at the file before

`tools/audit/derivation_exemplars/l0-l1/bwv1049_03_presto.mscx`, **1,701,770 bytes — the archive's own
recorded length — with on-disk `sha256 a4d9d897…fbac80` equal to the extraction's**, so the
no-byte-altered claim is true of the object as it stands, not merely of the copy operation. The
provenance record beside it carries all nine items. **Both were verified at the objects by this side.**

**One addition that strengthens §3v rather than qualifying it:** the Brandenburg archive's
`<programRevision>` is **`c1a5e4c`**, which has the shape of a revision hash — **not** the
`abc123456` placeholder §3v found in the ABC archive. The contrast is now between two observed values

***★ AND §3v CARRIED A FALSE CLAIM, WHICH THE BATCH FOUND AND THIS SIDE HAS RE-ESTABLISHED AT THE
REGISTRY.*** ***THE FORMER WORDING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"and both archives' registry rows carry a

**★ THE PATTERN IS NOW FOUR, AND THE FOURTH OCCURRED INSIDE THE ENTRY THAT NAMED IT.** §3t a locator
carried unopened; §3u the premise that a score file is a notational record; §3v's mtime gap estimated
by eye; and now §3v's registry sentence — **written while both rows were in front of this side, having
been read minutes earlier.** That last fact is the diagnosis: **this side is not failing to check. It
is checking, and then writing a GENERALISING SENTENCE from memory of the check.** All four are of one
shape — *a claim spanning more than one object, or stating a property of a class rather than of a
named object.*

**A candidate bound, offered for the user to rule and NOT adopted here.** *A sentence in a record that
spans more than one object, or that states a property of a class rather than of a named object, is
written from a re-read at the moment of writing — or is written per object instead.* **This side does
not adopt its own bound**, both because §3v already showed that naming the pattern does not stop it,
and because a self-imposed checking rule is exactly the kind of instrument #19 says must be
established rather than asserted.

**Left OWED, established and not acted on.** `.gitattributes` **carries no `*.mscx` rule at all** — the

**Two guard refusals, reported as routing rather than as obstacles.** The standing shell-read guard

**What this does NOT do.** **It stages nothing** — the exemplar exists in the tree and is not in front
