## 3z. Ruling 20 — SCORE METADATA IS TO BE BUILT, GATED ON SERVICE TO THE ULTIMATE OBJECTIVE

**The user's words, verbatim: "As long as such meta-data tagging is helping us towards the ultimate
objective - I think it should be done."** Given upon §3x and the tiered summary that followed it.

**THE RULING, read at its own terms: the tagging is TO BE DONE, and the condition is OPERATIVE rather
than decorative** — *"as long as it is helping us towards the ultimate objective"*. **So each tier
must show its service to maximum-precision inference (#4) before it is built, and a tier that cannot
is not built.** This side reads the ruling as authorizing the work and as **refusing a blanket
mandate**, and says so rather than taking the wider reading.

**The tiers, as put to the user, with what each rests on.**

- **TIER A — safe now, CHARTER-derived.** Straight off `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's L0 given-list and L1's
  *Publishes* block: fermatas, ties, pedal marks, repeats and voltas, double bars, rests, bar lines,
  distinct time signatures, distinct key signatures, invisible notes, non-sounding notes, and the
  staff / part / voice / bar / chord / note counts. **It leaks nothing: §5 and §9 ARE pack member (7),
  so the deriving session already holds every definition these tags rest on.**
- **TIER B — safe now, DEFECT-derived.** The three check questions of §3u and §3v: the `<Harmony>` /
  `<FiguredBass>` / `<StaffText>` counts, whole-staff pair identity, and `<museScore version>` /
  `<programVersion>` / `<programRevision>`; plus `sha256`, byte size and the licence metaTag. **Every
  entry exists because something actually went wrong.**
- **TIER C — safe, but the COUNTING RULE is declared before it runs.** Release-driven change points as
  a share of all change points; staggered entries; sounding share; ornament counts by subtype; grace
  notes, tremolo, cue notes; anacrusis; time-signature change positions; maximum simultaneity. **The
  judgment hides in the rule, not in the count** — Ruling 12's condition (i) governs, and each tag
  carries the name of the rule that produced it.
- **TIER D — CLOSED from this direction, and not a "not yet."** Modulation, cadence *kind*, cadential
  six-four, hemiola, phrase boundary, harmonic rhythm. Tagging them needs either **our own analyzer —
  our output selecting the material that tests our output** — or a published annotation, which Ruling
  12's option C declined. **A finished detail specification does not cure this; it IS the
  circularity.** The single path out: face (f)'s **cues** are charter-designed to be computable
  without knowing the tonality, so *cadence-cue configurations* becomes Tier C **once the derivation
  defines the cues** — which is also the repair for the item named next.

**★ FOUR BOUNDS, WHICH ARE WHAT MAKE IT SAFE RATHER THAN MERELY USEFUL.** **(1)** Generated by a
committed script and re-derivable, **never hand-maintained** (#19) — a hand-kept table drifts and is
then trusted. **(2)** One home, **not a sixth registry** (#6) — there are already five. **(3)** Counts
with their denominators, never a bare share (D-486's shape), and **a tag that does not discriminate is
recorded as non-discriminating** rather than kept as though informative — the P2 lesson. **(4) ★
WHOLE-CORPUS COUNTS SAY WHAT OUR CORPORA CANNOT TEST; THEY MUST NOT BE USED TO PICK EXEMPLARS.** This
protects **Ruling 15's** own separation of a *representativeness* claim from an *existence* claim:
corpus frequency would drift the set toward the typical score, and **the charter's holes are in the
rare case by construction** — face (a)'s material is rare precisely because four-part homophony does
not contain it.

**★ AND THE FINDING THE TIERS EXPOSED IN A STANDING RULING, OWED AND NOT BLOCKING. RULING 15 CONTAINS
AN ITEM THAT CANNOT BE CHECKED AS THAT RULING REQUIRES.** Its phenomena list names **"more than one
kind of cadence"** while the ruling requires *each score's claim checked at the file before it is
staged*. **Cadence KIND is Tier D — it cannot be counted without deciding cadences.** The repair is
the cue-based reading above, and it is available only **after** the derivation defines the cues — so
**Ruling 15 asks for a check that its own derivation is meant to make possible.** Ordered backwards.
**Not blocking: the cadence slot is not the one being filled.**

**Two things the instrument must never be mistaken for**, recorded with the ruling: a
**representativeness sampler** (bound 4), and a **coverage claim about music** — counting everything we
hold cannot see past what we hold, which is **D-201**'s own warning that very large scores are
expected to be more common than our corpora.

**What this does NOT do.** **It builds nothing today** — no script is written, no table is generated,
no registry is touched, and the work still needs a dispatch. It does not reopen the phase order:
**Tiers A and B are buildable now; Tier C belongs at measurement design**, because each entry needs its
rule declared and ruled. It amends no governing document, does not touch `docs/score_inventory.md`, and
does not repair Ruling 15's cadence item — that stays owed. **No decisions-register identity is
allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3aa. NOT A RULING — THE SWEEP RETURNS, AND IT REFUTED TWO OF THIS SIDE'S OWN CHECK QUESTIONS

**Not a ruling. The return of the slot sweep — 648 `.mscx` files over eight directories, 0 parse
failures, 0 rows failing reconciliation — and the correction of two defects in the check questions
THIS SIDE authored.** `cc_slot_sweep_report_2026_09_01.md` read whole; its checkable claims
re-established at the objects by this side. **The control, `bwv1049_03_presto.mscx`, reproduced every
one of its established values**, so the counting instrument is not wrong where it can be tested.

**★ THE SWEEP TESTED THIS SIDE'S TEST, AND THE TEST FAILED TWICE. THAT IS THE ENTRY'S HEADLINE.**

**(1) THE FERMATA COLUMN WOULD HAVE REPORTED THE GATE REPERTOIRE AS FERMATA-FREE.** The dispatch worded
it `<Fermata>`. **A fermata has two encodings in this population and they PARTITION it by corpus:** all
**361** chorales carry **zero** `<Fermata>` and write fermatas as
`<Articulation><subtype>fermataAbove</subtype>`; every non-chorale file does the opposite, 108 of them
carrying the element form, and **no file in the sweep carries both**. **Re-established at the object by
this side: `001 Aus meines Herzens Grunde.mscx` holds 0 `<Fermata>` and 12 `fermataAbove`.** Both are
now separate columns and neither is folded into the other. **This is the near-miss of the earlier
fermata grep recurring as a defect in a written instrument** — the same wrong pattern, this time
embedded where it would have produced a table.

**(2) `visible="0"` IS NOT HOW THIS FORMAT MARKS A NOTE INVISIBLE.** It is a child, `<visible>0</visible>`;
the attribute form appears in **zero of 648** files, established rather than assumed. The column as
worded would have read zero everywhere and been called non-discriminating, which would have been false.

**Both corrections ADD a count; neither removes one and neither substitutes a judgment for a count.**

**★★ AND THE THIRD DEFECT, WHICH IS THE LARGEST AND IS IN §3u ITSELF: THE PLAINNESS TEST EXCLUDES THE
GATE REPERTOIRE ON A TITLE.** §3u defined plainness over three limbs — `<Harmony>`, `<FiguredBass>`,
`<StaffText>` — all zero. **`harmony` is 0 in all 361 chorales and `figured_bass` is 0 in all 361. What
disqualifies them is `staff_text`, non-zero in all 361** — and **re-established at the object by this
side, `001 Aus meines Herzens Grunde.mscx`'s two `<StaffText>` items are `001 Aus meines Herzens
Grunde` and `(BWV 269; R 030)`: the piece's title and its catalogue identifiers.**

**The defect is this side's, and its shape is the same family again:** `<StaffText>` was put into the
test **by analogy with two annotation elements, without establishing what it carries.** ***THE FORMER
WORDING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"is this file plain (annotation elements present)"* **with `<StaffText>`
named as a third disqualifying limb.**

**A candidate repair, offered for the user and NOT adopted here.** *Plainness has TWO mechanical limbs —
`<Harmony>` 0 and `<FiguredBass>` 0. `<StaffText>` is not a limb: its DISTINCT STRINGS are reported so a
person can see whether any is analytical.* **That keeps the judgment with the user and out of the
table**, which is Ruling 20's own line, rather than replacing one mechanical test with a subtler one.
**This side does not adopt its own repair** — §3y already recorded that naming a pattern does not stop
it.

**★ EXACTLY ONE FILE OF 648 IS PLAIN, AND IT IS THE CONTROL.** `<Harmony>` is non-zero in **286 of 286**
non-chorale files — **Couperin 9/9, Scarlatti 69/69, Handel 6/6, C.P.E. Bach 66/66, the English and
French suites 89/89, Frescobaldi 47/47.** §3u's five-of-five pattern reproduces at **six of six corpora
and every single file.** **Not one non-chorale file in the sweep is free of `<Harmony>`** — so no plain
in-tree `.mscx` exists for the ornaments slot, and that slot cannot be filled the way the
staggered-entries slot was.

**★ THREE NON-DISCRIMINATING COLUMNS — AND A SCOPE QUALIFICATION THIS SIDE ADDS RATHER THAN INHERITS.**
`figured_bass`, `pedal` and `tremolo` are **zero in all 648 files**. **`<Pedal>` is named in
`FRAMEWORK.md` §5's L0 GIVEN-LIST** — a contracted input — and **tremolo is named in Ruling 15's face
(a)**. **★ BUT THE SWEEP COVERED SEVEN OF THE FORTY-FIVE DCML SUB-CORPORA, ALL BAROQUE OR CLASSICAL
KEYBOARD PLUS THE CHORALES. The finding is *nothing in the SWEPT SET exercises either*, and it must not
be restated as *nothing we hold*** — the romantic piano corpora this sweep did not touch are where pedal
marks would be expected. **The wider claim is unestablished and is not made.** Within that scope this is
**Ruling 20's bound 4 doing exactly what it was written for**: the table's answer is what our material
cannot test.

**Two further absences, per corpus.** **Ornaments are absent from all 361 chorales** — Ruling 15's own
stated reason for going beyond them, now confirmed at scale rather than reasoned. And Frescobaldi's 47
files carry **no repeats, no voltas, no grace chords and no invisible notes.**

**★ FACE (d) IS WELL SUPPLIED, WHICH IS THE ONE UNQUALIFIEDLY GOOD RESULT.** **30 files carry more than
one distinct time signature** — 20 Frescobaldi, 4 chorales (`011 Jesu, nun sei gepreiset`, `150 Welt
ade, ich bin dein müde`, `252 Jesu, nun sei gepreist`, `280 Eins ist not!`), 3 Scarlatti, 3 C.P.E. Bach —
against Ruling 15's cautious *"one carrying a meter change **if the tree holds one**"*. **The tree holds
thirty.** The richest are Frescobaldi's `12.47_Capriccio_sopra_la_Girolmeta` with 8 and `12.46_Bergamasca`
with 7. **21 distinct time signatures occur across the sweep**, and **156 files carry no `<KeySig>`
element at all.**

**★ THE WHOLE SWEPT POPULATION PASSES §3v's THIRD CHECK QUESTION, WHICH IS A POSITIVE FINDING.** Only
**two** `programVersion` values occur across 648 files — `3.0.0` (the control) and `3.6.2` (the other
647) — against this repository's `5.0.0`, re-established at `version.cmake` by the batch rather than
relayed. **No file in the sweep can be this build's output.** Two Scarlatti files, `K002.mscx` and
`K004.mscx`, carry an **empty** `<programRevision>` where the other 646 carry `3224f34`; **named,
verified at both objects, and no causal claim made** — their declared `3.6.2` already answers the check
question.

**Ruling 19's declared fallback is NOT plain, established at the file.**
`frescobaldi_fiori_musicali/MS3/12.15_Recercar_dopo_il_Credo.mscx` carries **162 `<Harmony>`** —
re-counted at the score file, and independently the same 162 this side counted before Ruling 19 —
`<FiguredBass>` 0, `<StaffText>` 3, 8 `<Fermata>`, 2 body staves, one time signature (4/2), 0 ornaments
and **0 grace chords**. **The fallback was always Option D and this confirms it: barring `<Harmony>` by
name is what it requires.**

**Two declared departures by the batch, recorded because a declared departure is worth more than a clean
report.** It ran the directory existence check first as a shell `ls`/`wc` over repository paths, noticed
that D-253 sends that read to the file tools, **and re-established the same counts through `Glob`** —
the reported figures are the ones two independent enumerations agree on. And it took one digest via
`python -c` with the path passed as `argv` rather than as a literal, **which the guard admits but which
is its declared ceiling**, and recorded using it rather than letting it pass. **The standing shell-read
guard itself fired twice**, naming D-253 and its substitutes both times.

**What this does NOT do.** **It selects nothing, ranks nothing and stages nothing** — the batch was
forbidden to, and this entry does not do it either. It amends no ruling: §3u's plainness limbs stand as
written until the user rules on the repair, and the sweep's two column corrections are recorded as
corrections to a **dispatch**, not to a ruling. It fills no slot. It does not restate the
non-discriminating finding beyond the swept scope. It touches no registry — `gen_score_tags.py` and
`score_tags_l0l1_sweep.json` are one generated artifact and the four existing registries gained nothing
(Ruling 20, bounds 1 and 2). **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot
accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3ab. Ruling 21 — how a decision surface is written: plainly, at length, and without invented words

**The user's words, verbatim: "decision-surfaces should be: fact based, weighed against ultimate
objective and guiding principles, in plain uncompressed language free from LLM-invented jargon etc."**
Given on the staged-set surface of 2026-09-01, before it was disposed of.

**The ruling.** A decision surface is written in **plain, uncompressed language**. It reasons on the page
rather than summarising a reasoning that happened elsewhere. It uses **no vocabulary this side invented**
for the occasion. The two requirements Ruling 1 already carries — that every alternative is fact-based,
and that every alternative is weighed on both the ultimate objective and the guiding principles — stand
unchanged; this ruling governs the **form** in which that weighing is set down.

**What it sharpens rather than replaces.** Ruling 1(b) already said that a rating which does not rate
does not discharge the requirement, and **D-424** already required the principle behind every pro and
con to be named. **This ruling says that a rating compressed into a stub does not rate either**, which
Ruling 1(b) implied and did not state.

**The evidence for it is the surface that prompted it, and the three defects are this side's.**

**First, it was compressed where it should have been spelled out.** Each option carried a two-line stub
on each axis. *"Strongest by a wide margin"* is an assertion, not a weighing — it names a conclusion and
withholds the reasoning that would let the user disagree with it. **And the principle behind most of the
pros and cons was not named at all, which D-424 requires.**

**Second, it used labels where it should have used language** — lettered options, figures stacked behind
punctuation, and tables standing where the reasoning belonged.

**Third, and this one is a defect of a kind the record already governs: it carried a RESERVED-WORD
COLLISION.** This side had been writing ***bar*** to mean *prohibit* — *"bar `<Harmony>` by name"*, *"the
bar's compliance"*. **`cowork_blind_session_brief_l0_l1.md` §0 reserves *bar* for the metric unit, never
*measure*.** In a surface that also reported a file's bar count, *"the bar covering both elements"*
collides outright. **The replacement word is *exclude*.**

**What is done about the collision, following this session's own precedent rather than inventing a
route.** **D-113's requirement is that no NEW collision is introduced.** The bare uses already standing
at four places in this record — the *bar-in-brief* option name and the fallback's wording among them —
are **inherited**, and inherited bare uses are the scoped terminology pass's business (**OI-229**), which
is exactly how this side handled bare *register* earlier in this sitting. **They are NOT rewritten here;
no dated entry is edited. From this ruling forward the word is *exclude*.**

**What this does NOT do.** It changes no ruling already given and reopens no surface already disposed of.
It does not amend Ruling 1, D-424 or D-599, all of which stand. It does not license rewriting the four
inherited uses, and it creates no open-items row — OI-229 already exists and this is one more instance
for it. It touches no governing document, no design point, no charter and no pack member. **No
decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3ac. Ruling 22 — the staged set is three files, and the keyboard file carries a condition

**The user's words, verbatim: "ok, let us go with your recommendation."** Given on the staged-set surface
as rewritten under Ruling 21.

**The ruling. The set staged for the L0+L1 deriving session is THREE files, not four**, because the
change of time signature comes with the chorale rather than costing a file of its own.

**One — the contrapuntal file, already in the tree.**
`tools/audit/derivation_exemplars/l0-l1/bwv1049_03_presto.mscx`, placed there under Ruling 19 with both
its conditions met. It covers staggered entries, and orchestral voicing where voice membership is a
notational convenience rather than a fact.

**Two — the chorale.** `tools/dcml/bach_chorales/MS3/011 Jesu, nun sei gepreiset.mscx` — 103,829 bytes,
66 bars, 417 chords, two staves, `<Harmony>` 0, `<FiguredBass>` 0, two time signatures (3/4 and 4/4),
twenty-three fermatas written as articulations, one end-repeat, three rests, five ties, `programVersion`
3.6.2. **It covers three of Ruling 15's phenomena at once** — the gate repertoire's own idiom, the change
of meter of face (d), and the fermatas and repeat of face (e) — **and it needs nothing excluded, because
it carries no analysis annotation.**

**Three — the keyboard file, with a condition that is part of the ruling.**
`tools/dcml/cpe_bach_keyboard/MS3/wq55n02a.mscx` — 552,240 bytes, 168 bars, 1,140 chords, two staves; six
kinds of ornament (16 mordents, 23 short trills, 2 tremblements, 19 turns, 1 inverted turn, 1 up-prall);
two grace-note encodings (10 sixteenth, 5 thirty-second); **244 cue-sized notes, 242 invisible notes and
five notes marked not sounding**; two start-repeats and two double bars; 167 `<Harmony>` and thirteen
`<StaffText>`.

- **It is staged as published, with `<Harmony>` AND `<StaffText>` EXCLUDED BY ELEMENT NAME in the
  brief**, and the exclusion's compliance recorded in the session's independence record.
- **THE CONDITION: the reason for its 242 invisible notes is established at the file BEFORE it is
  staged.** The count is established; the reason is not, and it is about a fifth of the file. If they are
  an editorial artifact of that edition rather than ordinary notation, the exemplar would be teaching the
  session about a quirk rather than about notation.
- **THE DECLARED FALLBACK, ruled now rather than improvised later:** if the condition fails, the slot goes
  to `tools/dcml/couperin_clavecin/MS3/05_cinquieme_prelude.mscx` — 280,063 bytes, 48 bars, 606 chords,
  two ornament kinds, two grace-note encodings, eight cue-sized notes, **`<Harmony>` 96 and `<StaffText>`
  0**, so only one element kind is excluded there.

**Why, on the objective.** The worth of an exemplar is the precision that would otherwise be lost on the
cases it carries: a notational case the session never meets is one it either fills with the most
plausible reading, which the phase forbids, or writes down as an open question and leaves unanswered.
**`wq55n02a` is the only file in the 648 swept that carries the second half of face (a)** — the brief
names *cue-sized notes, notes in a voice that is not sounding* among the cases that decide what makes a
note eligible, and invisible and non-sounding notes are almost absent from the other 647 files. **It also
touches L0's contract directly, that contract giving *whether each note sounds and is visible* as two
separate facts**, and this being the only exemplar where those two facts come apart in quantity.

**Why, on the principles.** The chorale needs no exclusion at all and is selected by a criterion that can
be re-run, so **Ruling 12's objection about the selecting party's hand does not attach.** The keyboard
file's exclusion is heavier than the fallback's — two element kinds and 180 items against one kind and
96 — and that matters because the exclusion relies on the session doing as it is told, which the brief's
own stop-on-meeting clause exists because it is not fully reliable. **The condition on the invisible
notes is the same shape as Ruling 19's condition (i): a checkable fact is checked before the file is
promised, rather than assumed** — and Ruling 19's whole-staff check is why that shape is now the default,
having saved a good exemplar from being discarded on a 24-chord window.

**Where the axes conflicted and what settled it.** The Couperin file and the empty-slot option rate
better on the principles axis; `wq55n02a` rates better on the objective axis. **Ruling 1(c) settles it:
the objective takes precedence.** It is worth recording how far apart they were: **on principles the gap
is one extra element kind in the exclusion instruction; on the objective it is a whole half of face (a)
that only one file carries.** The precedence rule confirmed what the sizes of the two gaps already
showed.

**The declined options, recorded because an excluded alternative is evidence about the choice.** **The
Couperin prelude alone** — cleanest instruction and smallest file, but it carries no invisible and no
non-sounding notes, so half of face (a) would go unmet; it is now the declared fallback rather than a
discarded option. **Both keyboard files** — fullest coverage, but four files rather than three and twice
the excluded material for the session to walk past. **Leaving the slot empty and declaring the gap** —
cleanest of all on principles, with Ruling 12's condition (iii) as precedent, but the worst on the
objective, since face (a) is the largest hole the charter deliberately leaves and the session was
chartered to fill it; it would have rested entirely on theory and published research with no notated case
to test any of it against.

**What the set cannot cover, ruled as declared gaps rather than left to be discovered.** **Tremolo
appears in none of the 648 files** and is named in face (a). **Pedal marks appear in none of them** and
`<Pedal>` is in `FRAMEWORK.md` §5's L0 given-list as a contracted input — **stated at its true scope: the
sweep covered seven of forty-five corpora, all Baroque and Classical keyboard plus the chorales, and the
romantic piano corpora that were not swept are where pedal marks would be expected.** **Cadence kinds**
cannot be counted at the file at all, which is Ruling 15's own unfulfillable item. **All three are
declared to the session, which then writes the want as an open question and does not fill it** — Ruling
12's condition (iii).

**What this does NOT do.** **It stages nothing** — staging is a later act, and the keyboard file is not
staged at all until its condition is met. It writes no brief and boots no session. It does not settle
whether `<StaffText>` is a disqualifying test or a reported string — **§3aa's plainness repair is still
unruled**, and the chorale is chosen on `<Harmony>` 0 and `<FiguredBass>` 0, which hold either way. It
amends no ruling, edits no score, and copies nothing. It touches no design point, no charter and no pack
member. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

**What follows mechanically, none of it executed here.** A dispatch to establish the reason for the
invisible notes, which is the condition. Then the brief's amendment — **it still says at lines 157 and
256 that no annotated scores are staged, which Ruling 15 superseded** — including the staging paragraph
and the exclusion instruction. Then the session is booted by the user with a fresh Cowork session, per
the 2026-08-26 role ruling.

## 3ad. Ruling 23 — the set is four files; the condition is met on corrected reasoning; and three figures of Ruling 22 were wrong

**The user's words, verbatim: "I agree on your rwcommendation."** Given on the revised recommendation
that followed his challenge to the weighing, and read here as covering that revision whole: **four
files, with `02_second_prelude` as the fourth.**

**THE RULING. The staged set is FOUR files.**

1. `tools/audit/derivation_exemplars/l0-l1/bwv1049_03_presto.mscx` — 1,701,770 bytes, staggered entries
   and orchestral voicing. Already in the tree under Ruling 19.
2. `tools/dcml/bach_chorales/MS3/011 Jesu, nun sei gepreiset.mscx` — 103,829 bytes, the gate idiom, a
   change of time signature, twenty-three fermatas and an end-repeat. Nothing excluded.
3. `tools/dcml/cpe_bach_keyboard/MS3/wq55n02a.mscx` — 552,240 bytes, six kinds of ornament, two
   grace-note encodings, and the only worked case in 648 files of a note that sounds without being
   visible. `<Harmony>` and `<StaffText>` excluded by element name.
4. `tools/dcml/couperin_clavecin/MS3/02_second_prelude.mscx` — 143,156 bytes, sixteen appoggiaturas and
   twenty-four short trills, `<Harmony>` 48 and `<StaffText>` 0. `<Harmony>` excluded by element name.

**Ruling 22's condition on the invisible notes is MET, and the reasoning by which it is met is corrected
rather than stretched.** The batch established that all 242 are concentrated in 24 of 84 bars, never in
voice 1, never tied, never alone at their moment, all cue-sized and stemless, and that **every one of the
242 has a visible note of the same pitch already sounding when it starts.** 237 of them sound while not
being visible. ***THE FORMER REASONING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"If they are an editorial artifact of that
edition rather than ordinary notation, the exemplar would be teaching the session about a quirk rather
than about notation."* **That premise was too narrow, and the facts refuted it rather than satisfying
it.** L0's contract gives *whether each note sounds* and *whether it is visible* as **two separate
facts**, and that pair is idle unless files exist where the two come apart. **This is the only file in
648 where they come apart in quantity.** An analyzer reading all 1,290 notes rather than the 1,048 a
reader sees gets 24 of 84 bars wrong, with up to twenty spurious onsets in one bar. **The artifact is the
case the contract anticipates, not a distraction from it.**

**★ AND THIS SIDE'S WEIGHING OF THE FOUR-FILE OPTION WAS WEAK, WHICH THE USER'S CHALLENGE EXPOSED. THE
THREE REASONS GIVEN AGAINST IT ARE RECORDED AS FAILED.** ***THE FORMER WORDING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"it
makes four files rather than three, presses hardest on Ruling 15's constraint that the set stays small,
and doubles the excluded material the session must walk past."*

- ***"Four rather than three"* is not a reason.** **Ruling 15's own words are *"about four scores."*** Three
  was a saving this side invented when the chorale absorbed the meter change, and then treated as a
  constraint.
- ***"Presses hardest on the size constraint"* was asserted without measurement.** The fourth file adds
  **143,156 bytes to 2,357,839 — about 6 per cent** — and **286 chords to 7,918, under 4 per cent.**
- ***"Doubles the excluded material"* is wrong twice.** It is **48 items against 180 — about 27 per cent,
  not double** — and it **introduces NO new element kind**, `<Harmony>` being excluded already. The
  instruction does not grow more complex; one count grows.

**And the check this side should have run first, run only after the challenge: the OVERLAP.** Couperin
`05`'s two ornament kinds are both among `wq55n02a`'s six; its `grace16` is already there; its eight
cue-sized notes stand against 244. **Its only non-redundant contribution is the APPOGGIATURA encoding** —
which is a real one, appoggiatura and `grace16` not being the same kind of thing, one taking its time
from the note it leans on and the other not, which is face (a)'s own question. **And once the fourth file
is chosen FOR that one contribution, `05` is the wrong Couperin: `02_second_prelude` carries sixteen
appoggiaturas against seventeen at HALF the bytes and HALF the exclusions.** The fourth file is therefore
`02`, and `05` is neither staged nor a fallback.

***★ THREE FIGURES OF RULING 22 WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED HERE.*** The sweep's `measures` column counts
`<Measure>` **elements**, one per staff per bar, and this side read it as bars. ***THE FORMER WORDING,
PRESERVED (#12):*** Ruling 22 records the keyboard file at *"168 bars"* and the chorale at *"66 bars"*.
**The keyboard file has 84 bars; the chorale has 33; and `02_second_prelude` has 19, not the 38 its row
shows.** The batch found this by reading the generator, **which this dispatch had ordered it to do — so
the instruction found the error, not this side's checking.** Every other figure of Ruling 22 re-established
at the file without divergence.

**Why, on the two axes, in short because the surface carries it at length.** On the **objective**: the
set now covers staggered entries, orchestral voicing, the gate idiom, a change of meter, fermatas and a
repeat, six ornament kinds, three grace-note encodings, cue-sized notes, and the visible-versus-sounding
split — and the fourth file is insurance in place rather than a contingency, should the invisible layer
absorb the session's attention. On the **principles**: every file is checked on all three questions of
§3u and §3v; no file is edited, stripped or derived; the exclusions name two element kinds across the
whole set and add no third; and **Ruling 15's constraint that the set stays small is measured rather than
asserted** — which is what the failed weighing above did not do.

**★ AND ONE PRACTICAL CONSTRAINT THIS SIDE HAS NOT ESTABLISHED, RAISED HERE BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME SHAPE
AS RULING 15's CONSTRAINT (iii) AND WAS NEVER ASKED.** **The four files total 2,500,995 bytes of XML.**
Measured: `wq55n02a` is **18,510 lines at 552,240 bytes**, and chorale 001 is **2,139 lines at 58,371
bytes** — so this format runs about **28 to 30 bytes per line**, putting the set near **eighty-four
thousand lines**, of which the Brandenburg alone is about two thirds. **Whether a deriving session can
read that at all is UNESTABLISHED.** Ruling 15's constraint (iii) states that a practical constraint must
be established before anything is promised, and this side applied that discipline to whether a `.mscz`
could be read and **did not apply it to whether the staged set can be.** **No token or context figure is
asserted here** — the bytes and the two line counts are established and the readability question is not.
**It is raised as owed, and it is owed BEFORE the brief's staging paragraph is written**, because a
staging paragraph promises the session files it may not be able to hold.

**What this does NOT do.** **It stages nothing** — staging is a later act. It does not open
`02_second_prelude`, which **no one has yet read at the file**; everything known about it is one row of a
generated table, and that table's own run found two of its column definitions wrong. It writes no brief
and boots no session. It does not settle the plainness repair, the `.gitattributes` question, or Ruling
15's cadence item. It edits no score. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register
cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3ae. Ruling 24 — quality is worth more than volume of work, and the four files stand

**The user's words, verbatim: "In general, quality = ultimate objective, is more important than volume
of work."** Given on the volume finding, before any option had been weighed.

**THE RULING, general and standing. Where a smaller amount of work and a better answer point in
different directions, the better answer wins.** The cost of doing the work is not a reason to accept a
lesser result. **This sits beside Ruling 1(c) rather than replacing it:** 1(c) settles a conflict between
the objective and the guiding principles; **this settles a conflict between the objective and effort**,
which 1(c) did not reach and which the record did not previously carry.

**What it disposes of here, immediately and against this side's own suggestion.** This side had raised
swapping the Brandenburg presto for a Frescobaldi ricercar at about an eighth of its size. **That option
had NO quality argument whatever** — it loses orchestral voicing across nine staves, which is L0's
contract and face (a), and gains nothing but a smaller file. **It was a trade of the objective against
effort, which is exactly what this ruling forbids, and it is withdrawn.** ***The former suggestion,
preserved (#12):*** *"or swap it for a ricercar, lose orchestral voicing, and bring the load to about 1.5
MB."*

**★ AND ONE DISTINCTION THIS SIDE MUST NOT HIDE BEHIND THE RULING, BECAUSE THE TWO THINGS ARE NOT THE
SAME.** *Volume of work* is how much effort a task costs us. *Volume the deriving session must read* is
whether it can do the job at all. **A session that cannot hold its material does not produce a worse
answer more slowly; it produces a broken one.** Ruling 24 disposes of the first and does not reach the
second. **But the second is UNESTABLISHED and this side will not trade against a constraint it has not
measured** — which is what the ricercar suggestion did.

**The measured facts, established at the objects and not estimated.** Lines counted at each file:
Brandenburg **64,500** lines / 1,701,770 bytes · `wq55n02a` **18,510** / 552,240 · Couperin 02 **4,802** /
143,156 · chorale 011 **3,768** / 103,829 — **91,580 lines and 2,500,995 bytes of score.** The `l0-l1`
boot pack's ten files total **664,441 bytes**; the brief is 22,704. **The session's whole read is about
3,188,000 bytes, of which the scores are 78 per cent and the Brandenburg alone is 53 per cent.**

**The only precedent, and it gives no datum on scores.** The pilot's whole read was the `scoring-model`
pack plus its brief — **501,986 + 20,128 = 522,114 bytes** — and **that pack staged no score at all**,
its brief stating in terms that *"this brief and that directory are the session's whole read inside this
repository."* **So the L0+L1 session would read about six times what the pilot read, and no deriving
session in this project has ever been given a score.** Ruling 12's *"pilot's two standing bars"* were
precautionary rules carried across, not evidence that a score was ever staged.

**THE DISPOSITION, following the ruling rather than hedging against an unmeasured fear. All four files
stand, and none is dropped or shrunk to reduce the read.** Two things ride with that:

- **The brief permits a partial read and requires it recorded.** The session is told it need not read
  every file whole and **must record what it read of each**, which the independence record already
  collects. **It is NOT told what any file is for** — naming the phenomenon a file was staged for would
  point at the conclusion the session is chartered to derive, which is the objection Ruling 12 upheld
  against its own option D. **A bound on method is not a pointer at content.**
- **The measured-cost record becomes the establishment.** The brief's §5 already requires the session to
  record which pack members and which sources each statement consulted, and which members no statement
  consulted at all. **That is how the real budget gets established for the next subject, instead of being
  guessed at before this one.**

**The residual risk, stated rather than argued away.** A session may read too little and derive from
less than it was given, and a partial read recorded is a fact after the event rather than a guard before
it. **This side has no way to establish capacity in advance and says so** instead of choosing a smaller
set to feel safer.

**What this does NOT do.** It stages nothing and changes no file of the set. It does not amend Ruling
1(c), D-424 or D-599. It does not license reading less of anything — the permission it names is the
session's and is recorded, not this side's. It does not settle the plainness repair, the
`.gitattributes` question, or Ruling 15's cadence item. **No decisions-register identity is allocated** —
that register cannot accept one and `cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3af. NOT A RULING — THE SITTING LANDED; THIS SIDE'S CLAIM WAS FALSE; AND THIS SIDE'S OWN DISPATCH CARRIED A DT-26

**Not a ruling. The return of the landing batch, and two corrections of this side's work.**
`cc_sitting_landing_report_2026_09_01.md` read whole; its checkable claims re-established at the objects
by this side.

**★ THIS SIDE'S FLAGGED CLAIM WAS FALSE, AND THE FLAG IS THE ONLY REASON IT WAS CAUGHT AT THE RIGHT
PLACE.** This side told the user the ruling record *"has never landed in git."* **It is tracked.** HEAD's
tree held blob `d02c571a…` at **16,630 bytes**; the working copy pinned at `78b4154e…` is **185,646
bytes**; the difference is **2,083 lines added and 0 removed — a pure append.** So what had not landed
was this sitting's additions, not the record. **The three preceding batches' `M` listings were right and
the prose was wrong**, which is what the dispatch's §0 flag said would need establishing. ***THE FORMER
WORDING, PRESERVED (#12):*** *"the sitting record is 185 KB carrying Rulings 1 through 24, and it has
never landed in git."*

**The landing itself.** Commit `e02d982c158d1899da67c5acf1d73478edc6df0b`, parent
`f54995c092…` — the same HEAD Task 1 established, so nothing was rebased beneath it. **29 paths**, every
one named by the dispatch and none outside it, checked in both directions from git. Not pushed, not
amended, nothing tagged. **The branch is `master`, and neither `CLAUDE.md` nor the dispatch protocol
states any branch rule** — the batch reported that its own generic harness default said to branch first,
that the dispatch forbade branching on its own judgment, and that it therefore committed where it stood.
**The `cc_*` committed-material convention is SILENT** at `CLAUDE.md`, at the dispatch protocol and at
`DECISIONS.md`; `.gitignore` speaks only by exclusion and does not exclude root-level `cc_*`. The batch
committed them as instructed and flagged the silence.

**One correction of authority worth carrying.** The batch established that **D-230's verbatim is the
decisions register's rule (c) and says nothing about a sitting record** — the interim-carrier clause is
the dispatch protocol's own, and citing D-230 alone for this landing would be the shape **D-643**
forbids. **This side had cited D-230 for it repeatedly, including in the dispatch.** Recorded as a
mis-citation corrected at the object.

***★ AND THIS SIDE'S OWN DISPATCH CARRIED A DT-26 — A SCOPE STATED IN PROSE AND ENUMERATED BY A NARROWER
PATTERN.*** Its §2 said *"this sitting's dispatches and their reports"* and then gave the pattern

**★ AND THE SAME OMISSION REACHES FOUR MORE OF THIS SITTING'S PRODUCTS, WHICH THE DISPATCH DID NOT NAME

**What this does NOT do.** It stages nothing and boots no session. It adds no ignore rule and does not

## 3ag. NOT A RULING — THE SECOND PASS CLOSED, AND THE ENUMERATION DEFECT RECURRED IN THIS SIDE'S REPAIR OF IT

**Not a ruling. The return of the second landing pass, and a third instance of the same defect — this
time in the document written to correct the second.** `cc_sitting_landing_second_report_2026_09_01.md`
read whole.

**The commit.** `98f53aaa723b143dc557ea7785b9d39d112cd114`, parent `e02d982c…` — HEAD confirmed at the
object before anything rested on it, so nothing was rebased beneath it. **Ten paths, every one named and
every one found**, so the *reported-not-hunted-for* clause was never reached. **The arithmetic
reconciles without an adjustment term: 857 records before, 847 after, ten cleared** — exact this time
because all ten were individual file records, where the first pass had one directory record standing for
ten pack members. The exclusion held: both excluded files carry the same git blob identifiers before and
after, and both are still untracked. `.gitattributes` untouched. Not pushed, not amended.

***★ AND THE DEFECT RECURRED IN THE REPAIR. THE SECOND DISPATCH DID NOT NAME ITS OWN DISPATCH.*** §2
listed ten paths and **`cc_instruction_sitting_landing_second_2026_09_01.md` was not among them**, so the
batch — correctly, since §6 made an unnamed path a STOP — left it untracked and said so. **The first pass
had committed its own dispatch only because its date-bearing pattern happened to reach it. The second
pass used no pattern, which was the point, and the accident that had covered the gap was gone.** That is
**three instances in three documents**: §2's prose-versus-pattern gap, the four `tools/audit/` artifacts
it did not name, and now this.

**★ THE SHAPE IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT, IT IS A RECURSION, AND NAMING IT IS WORTH MORE THAN A THIRD PASS.**
**Every landing pass creates two artifacts the next pass must land: its own dispatch and its own
report.** A pass can commit its own dispatch, because that file exists before the commit; it can never
commit its own report, which does not exist until after. **So the steady state is two owed files per
pass, and a pass that forgets its own dispatch leaves three.**

**A candidate rule, offered for the user and NOT adopted here.** *A landing dispatch names, at minimum:
its own dispatch; the previous pass's report; and whatever else is owed. Its own report is left to the
next pass, and the dispatch says so.* **That closes at two files per pass instead of growing**, and it
is a rule about enumeration rather than another act. **This side does not adopt its own rule** — §3y and
§3aa both record that naming a pattern of this kind has not stopped it, and a self-imposed rule is the
class #19 says must be established rather than asserted.

**★ AND NO THIRD PASS IS RUN FOR IT.** Three files are now owed to the next landing — the second
dispatch, the second report, and the ruling record carrying §3af and this entry. **The dispatch
protocol's interim-carrier clause already provides for exactly that: things land at the NEXT dispatch's
Task 0.** Running a batch per file would be volume of work spent against no gain in the answer, which
**Ruling 24** disposes of. **The three are recorded as owed and left.**

**★ A VISIBLE CHANGE OF FORM IN THE GIT LOG, WHICH THE USER SHOULD MEET HERE.** The repository's
established commit trailer, **read at the preceding commit object rather than recalled**, ends
`Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`. **The dispatch named the string
without the parenthetical**, and since neither `CLAUDE.md` nor the dispatch protocol states any trailer
rule, the batch used the dispatch's block verbatim. **So the trailer's wording visibly changes at commit
`98f53aaa…`, and a new `Claude-Session` trailer appears there for the first time.** Both are stated
rather than left to be found in the log. **Whether the repository standardises on one form is the
user's**; nothing here is amended, because amending a commit to fix a trailer is not worth rewriting
history for.

**One new fact about the standing shell-read guard, recorded because it is the second time this session
the guard's reach beyond the repository has been observed.** It refused a `grep` aimed at a file in the
session scratchpad — **outside the repository** — reading the shell variable that held the path as a
repository path. **That is the guard denying on indeterminate, which is standing policy (D-647)**, and
the file tools were used instead, which is what the rule asks for anyway. **Five consecutive batches have
now met the guard's refusal of `git status --porcelain`**, each taking it as routing rather than as an
obstacle.

**What this does NOT do.** It stages nothing and boots no session. It runs no third pass. It amends no
commit and changes no trailer. It adds no ignore rule and does not edit `.gitattributes` — **that
question is unchanged and still the user's**. It does not adopt the enumeration rule it proposes. **No
decisions-register identity is allocated** — that register cannot accept one and
`cowork_register_rule_c_suspension_2026_08_28.md` is the route.

## 3ah. NOT A RULING — THE HANDOVER FILE IS CURRENT, AND THE BLIND SESSION STOPPED AT ITS OWN BOOT

**Two returns, recorded together because the second is the live one.**

**The prepend closed.** `cc_handoff_prepend_report_2026_09_01.md` read whole. `cowork_handoff.md` was

`4c9b7af5066fdf51e4b726f6fdc151b7e4153b0c`, four authorised paths. **Four of the seven entry files
turned out already tracked and unmodified**, so only three were committed. The proofs are the strong
kind: each span **located by searching for its own source file's bytes**, its digest equal to that
file's; the before-size fetched content-addressed from git rather than from the batch's own reading;
and **removing the inserted block reconstructs the original exactly**, which is insertion-only proven
rather than asserted.

**One finding surfaced and not repaired.** Six of the seven entry files end with a single newline
rather than a blank line, so **six of the twenty-four boundaries carry no blank line before their
`---`**, where all seventeen pre-existing boundaries do. Repairing it means adding bytes no source
file holds, which the dispatch forbade in terms. **The batch did not measure what a renderer does
with a `---` that follows a non-blank line and did not assert it** — correctly. *This side notes,
labelled as a reading of the CommonMark specification and not a measurement, that such a line is a
setext heading underline rather than a separator.* **Six single-byte insertions close it; it rides
with the next landing rather than earning a batch of its own** (Ruling 24 disposes of a batch per
byte).

**★ AND THE LIVE ONE: THE BLIND L0+L1 SESSION STOPPED AT ITS OWN BOOT AND IS WAITING.** It reported
that **the project memory index is injected at the boot of every Cowork session in this project,
before the session's first act and before it can read its brief.** **This side then read that index
at the object and the exposure is worse than the session's own account:** one entry states, in its
one-line summary, that user-written analytical content is banned as analyzer input while **key
signature, time signature, ties and pedal are ALLOWED, key signature as a weak prior** — which is
**four of L0's thirteen given items and its central assumption clause**, handed over before the
session acted. Two further entries reach voice membership as given input and the rejection of a
per-onset alternative. **That is not adjacent to L0's subject; it is L0's answer.**

**Three consequences, none of them ruled here.** **(i)** The phase definition's NOT ALLOWED clause —
*"implementation-derived material in the deriving sessions"* — is breached, so this is not a judgment
about tolerable leak. **(ii)** P1 barred `CLAUDE.md`; **the project keeps implementation knowledge in
at least three channels a session can meet** — that document, the injected memory index, and the code
— and **P1 barred one of three.** **(iii) The pilot ran under the same mechanism**, most memory
entries predating it; **this side cannot establish what the index held on that day and does not
assert that the pilot was polluted** — but its independence has never been checked against this
channel, and that reaches a completed and ratified piece of work.

**What was established about turning it off, since this side owed that answer and had wrongly put it
back to the user.** There **is** a control — **Settings → Memory → Pause memory** — and it is
**account-global, not per-project and not per-session**; **Reset** would permanently delete project
memories and must not be used. Two open feature requests on the Claude Code tracker ask for exactly
the finer control that does not exist. **But the documented wording is that pause stops memory being
*used* and *made*, which is not established to cover the boot injection** — that is the unestablished
shape #19 governs, and **it is cheaply testable**: pause, open a throwaway session in this project,
ask it as its first question what it received.

**★ AND THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST FOR THE METHOD.** The leak was not a file — **it was a one-line
index summary that states its file's CONCLUSION.** The file itself was never opened. **So the index
lines, not the memory files, are the channel**, and rewriting them to name a topic without stating its
answer is a small, reversible, mechanical repair that costs a reading session almost nothing.
**Recorded as this side's proposal, not adopted.**

**What this does NOT do.** It rules nothing, boots nothing, and repairs neither the six boundaries nor
the memory index. It does not touch `CLAUDE.md`, which **this side separately verified does carry both

and gates** — so P1's ground is true, verified at the file, and understated; **this side had asserted
